. The classical (covariant) Dieudonné theory establishes an equivalence

\Bigg\{\begin{aligned} &p\textnormal{-divisible groups}\\
&\textnormal{of finite height over $k$}\end{aligned}\Bigg\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Bigg\{\begin{aligned} &W(k)\textnormal{-modules free of finite rank equipped with operators $F,V$}\\
&\textnormal{such that $F$ is $\sigma$-linear, $V$ is $\sigma^{-1}$-linear, and $FV=VF=p$}.\end{aligned}\Bigg\}

(92)

For
p
-torsion groups, we have

\Bigg\{\begin{aligned} \textnormal{finite }p\textnormal{-torsion groups over $k$}\end{aligned}\Bigg\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Bigg\{\begin{aligned} &\textnormal{finite dimensional }k\textnormal{-vector spaces equipped with operators $F,V$}\\
&\textnormal{such that $F$ is $\sigma$-linear, $V$ is $\sigma^{-1}$-linear, and $FV=VF=0$}.\end{aligned}\Bigg\}.

(93)

Let
D
denote the (covariant) functor sending a
p
-divisible group (resp. a
p
-torsion group) over
k
to its associated
W(k)
-module (resp.
k
-vector space) in the target category. The functor
D
satisfies the relation

D(G[p])=D(G)/pD(G).

(94)

Here,
G[p]
denotes the kernel of the multiplication-by-
p
morphism on the
p
-divisible group
G
.

Remark 4.1
.

In general, a finite
p
-torsion group over
k
need not arise as the
p
-torsion subgroup of a
p
-divisible group. Specifically, the subcategory consisting of "finite groups which are the
p
-torsion of a
p
-divisible group" corresponds to
k
-vectors spaces in the target category which satisfies
\textnormal{Ker }F=\textnormal{Im }V
,
\textnormal{Ker}V=\textnormal{Im }F
. See
[
39
]
for an explanation.

Let
A/k
be an abelian variety. Let
\tilde{D}(A)=D(A[p^{\infty}])
denote the Dieudonné module of the
p
-divisible group associated to
A
and let
D(A)=D(A[p])
. There is a natural comparison isomorphism

\tilde{D}(A)/p\tilde{D}(A)\simeq D(A)\simeq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/k).

(95)

Recall that in our moduli problem,
A
is equipped with an action of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. This induces a corresponding action of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
on the Dieudonné modules
\tilde{D}(A)
and
{D}(A)
, leading to the natural decomposition

\tilde{D}(A)=\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq N}\big(\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}\oplus\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}\big).

(96)

The relative Frobenius and Verschiebung maps on
A/k
induce
W(k)
-linear maps
V:\tilde{D}(A)\to\tilde{D}(A^{(p)})
and
F:\tilde{D}(A^{(p)})\to\tilde{D}(A)
respectively, which further induces maps
V
and
F
(note the order!) on each component:

V:\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}\longrightarrow\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i+1}},\qquad F:\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i+1}}\longrightarrow\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}.

(97)

A similar decomposition holds for the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}
-components and for the Dieudonné module
D(A)
. Furthermore,
A
is equipped with a prime-to-
p
quasi-polarization
\lambda
, which induces a perfect pairing

\langle\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}},\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}\rangle_{\lambda}\longrightarrow W(k).

(98)

This pairing satisfies
\langle Fx,y\rangle_{\lambda}^{\sigma}=\langle x,Vy\rangle_{\lambda}
for
x\in\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
and
y\in\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i+1}^{c}}
. The subscript
\lambda
is omitted when it is clear in the context. For any free
W(k)
-submodule
M\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
, we define

M^{\perp}:=\big\{y\in\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}|\ \langle x,y\rangle\in pW(k)\ \textnormal{for all }x\in M\big\}.

(99)

By definition,
\omega_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
and
\omega_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}
are orthogonal complements of each other. As in Section 3, we simplify our notations by writing
\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
and
{D}(A)_{i}
for
\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
and
D(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
respectively.

An isogeny
f:A\rightarrow B
induces a homomorphism
\tilde{D}(A)\rightarrow\tilde{D}(B)
and also for each
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
- and
\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}
-component, compatible with
F
and
V
. When Ker
f\subseteq A[p]
, this induces
p\tilde{D}(B)\subseteq f(\tilde{D}(A))\subseteq\tilde{D}(B)
. Alternatively, this can be interpreted as an inclusion of lattices
\tilde{D}(A)\subseteq\tilde{D}(B)
in the
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]
-vector space
\tilde{D}(A)[\frac{1}{p}]\allowbreak=\tilde{D}(A)\otimes_{W(k)}W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]\allowbreak\simeq\tilde{D}(B)[\frac{1}{p}]
. The dual isogeny
g
of
f
, then induces an inclusion
g:\tilde{D}(B)\hookrightarrow\frac{1}{p}\tilde{D}(A)
.

4.2 Geometric description of strata

The following theorem, which corresponds to the
m=1
case in
[
23
]
, will provide a tool for constructing auxiliary Shimura varieties.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
k
be a perfect field containing the residue field
\mathcal{O}_{F}/(p)
, and let
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota)\in X(k)
be a
k
-point. Suppose that we are given a free
W(k)
-submodule
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq N
, which satisfies

p\tilde{D}(A)_{i}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{i},\quad V(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i})\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i+1},\quad\textnormal{and}\quad F(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i+1})\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}

(100)

for all
i
. Then there exists a unique pair
(B,\phi)
satisfying

(1)

B
is an abelian variety over
k
, endowed with an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-action
\iota_{B}:\mathcal{O}_{E}\to\textnormal{End}(B)
and a prime-to-
p
polarization
\lambda_{B}
, such that the Rosati involution associated with
\lambda_{B}
induces the complex conjugation on
\mathcal{O}_{E}
.

(2)

\phi:B\to A
is an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-equivariant isogeny, such that for
1\leq i\leq N
, the inclusion
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\hookrightarrow\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
is identified with the map
\phi_{\ast,i}:\tilde{D}(B)_{i}\rightarrow\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
induced by
\phi
, and
\phi^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ\phi=p\lambda_{B}
.

(3)
If
\textnormal{length}_{W(k)}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}/p\tilde{D}(A)_{i})=l_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq N
, then

\textnormal{dim }\omega_{B^{\vee}/k,i}=m_{i}-l_{i}+l_{i-1}.

(101)

(4)
The abelian variety
B/k
is equipped with a prime-to-
p
level structure
\eta_{B}
on
B
such that
\eta=\phi\circ\eta_{B}
.

Proof:
By our previous discussion, the polarization
\lambda
induces a perfect pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}:\allowbreak\tilde{D}(A)_{i}\times\tilde{D}(A)_{i^{c}}\allowbreak\to W(k)
for all
1\leq i\leq N
. Let
\tilde{E}_{i^{c}}:=\tilde{E}_{i}^{\perp}
be the orthogonal complement of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
. One can verify that

p\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i^{c}}\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}},\quad V(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i^{c}})\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{{(i+1)}^{c}},\quad\textnormal{and}\quad F(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{(i+1)^{c}})\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i^{c}}.

(102)

Thus, the submodule

\tilde{D}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\oplus\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i^{c}}\big)

(103)

is a free
W(k)
-submodule of
\tilde{D}(A)
equipped with an action of
F
and
V
. By Dieudonné theory, the quotient
\tilde{D}/p\tilde{D}(A)
corresponds to a closed subgroup scheme
H\subseteq A[p]
. Moreover, it follows from the above construction that
H=H^{\perp}
, meaning that
H
is maximal isotropic. Let
B:=A/H
, and let
\psi:A\to B
be the natural quotient. We define
\phi:B\to A
as the quotient with kernel
\psi(A[p])
. We now verify the properties of
(B,\phi)
.

(1) The
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-action follows from the construction. Since
H\subseteq A[p]
is maximal totally isotropic, it follows from
[
40
]
that there is a prime-to-
p
polarization
\lambda_{B}
on
B
with
\phi^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ\phi=p\lambda_{B}
.

(2) By construction of
\phi
, The Dieudonné module
\tilde{D}(B)
is identified with
\tilde{D}
, and this identification holds componentwise for each
\tau_{i}
.

(3) We have

\displaystyle\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \omega_{B^{\vee}/k,i}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \frac{V\tilde{D}(B)_{i-1}}{p\tilde{D}(B)_{i}}=\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \frac{V\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i-1}}{p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}

(104)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \frac{V\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i-1}}{Vp\tilde{D}(A)_{i-1}}+\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \frac{Vp\tilde{D}(A)_{i-1}}{p^{2}\tilde{D}(A)_{i}}-\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \frac{p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}{p^{2}\tilde{D}(A)_{i}}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{dim}_{k}\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i-1}}{p\tilde{D}(A)_{i-1}}-\textnormal{dim}_{k}\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}{p\tilde{D}(A)_{i}}+\textnormal{dim}_{k}\ \omega_{i}

\displaystyle=m_{i}-l_{i}+l_{i-1}.

(4) Since
\phi
is a
p
-isogeny, the prime-to-
p
level structure
\eta_{B}
on
B
is uniquely determined by
\eta
on
A
.
\hfill\square

Definition 4.3
.

Let
A
be an abelian scheme over
k
and
\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
be a tuple of free
W(k)
-submodules of
\tilde{D}(A)
. We call it an
F,V
-chain in
\tilde{D}(A)
if it satisfies condition (
100
).

Similarly, let
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
be a tuple of subspaces of
D(A)
. We call it an
F,V
-chain in
D(A)
if there exists an
F,V
-chain in
\tilde{D}(A)
, namely,
\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\}
, such that
\mathcal{E}_{i}=\allowbreak\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}/p\tilde{D}(A)_{i}\allowbreak\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/k)_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq N
.

Definition 4.4
.

A morphism of
k
-schemes
f:Y\to Z
is called a
Frobenius factor
if there exists
r\in\mdmathbb{N}
and a morphism
g:Z^{(p^{r})}\to Y
, such that
g\circ f:Z^{(p^{r})}\to Z
is the
p^{r}
-th power Frobenius morphism.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
f:Y\to Z
be a proper morphism between
k
-schemes of finite type, such that
Y
is reduced and
Z
is normal. If
f
is a bijection on closed points, then
f
is a Frobenius factor.

Proof.
This is
[
24
, Prop 4.8]
.
\hfill\square

In the following theorem, we construct correspondences between (strata of partial flag spaces over) Shimura varieties.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
B\subseteq P_{0}\subseteq P
be an intermediate parabolic subgroup such that the associated partial flag space
Y_{P_{0}}
is defined by recording at most one subbundle
\mathcal{E}_{r_{i}}^{i}\subseteq\omega_{i}
of rank
r_{i}
for each
i
(
r_{i}
can be
m_{i}
, in which case the moduli problem does not record any refinement at the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
-component). Let
Z
be the closed subscheme of
Y_{P_{0}}
defined by the condition

\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}_{r_{i+1}}^{i+1,(p)}),\qquad F(\mathcal{E}_{r_{i+1}}^{i+1,(p)})\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}

(105)

for all
1\leq i\leq N
(Here, the second condition is in fact empty, since
\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1}}\subseteq\omega^{(p)}_{i+1}=\textnormal{Ker }F
, but we keep it for uniformity). Then
Z
is, up to some Frobenius factor, isomorphic to a stratum
Z^{\prime}
in a partial flag space
Y^{\prime}_{P_{0}^{\prime}}
over
X^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime}
is the special fiber of a unitary Shimura variety with hyperspecial level structure and signature
(m_{i}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime})_{i=1}^{N}
, where

m_{i}^{\prime}=m_{i}-r_{i}+r_{i-1},\quad n_{i}^{\prime}=n-m_{i}^{\prime},\quad\textnormal{for $1\leq i\leq N$}.

(106)

Moreover, this correspondence respects additional structures.

Remark 4.7
.

The precise meaning of "respects additional structures" will be clarified in the proof. Specifically, if there is another intermediate parabolic subgroup
B\subseteq P_{1}\subseteq P_{0}\subseteq P
such that
Y_{P_{1}}
is given by further recording some
\mathcal{E}^{u}_{v}
’s that refine the Hodge filtration in the moduli problem, and
Z_{1}
is the pullback of
Z
under the natural projection, then
Z_{1}
is, up to some Frobenius factor, isomorphic to a stratum
Z_{1}^{\prime}
in
Y^{\prime}_{P_{1}^{\prime}}
over
Z^{\prime}\subseteq Y^{\prime}_{P_{0}^{\prime}}
for some parabolic subgroup
B^{\prime}\subseteq P_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq P_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq P^{\prime}
.

Proof.
It is easy to check that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}^{\prime}
.
By
[
24
, Corollary 8.2]
, there exists an
n
-dimensional vector space
V^{\prime}
over
E
equipped with a symplectic pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}\to\mdmathbb{Q}
satisfying

\qquad\langle\alpha x,y\rangle^{\prime}=\langle x,\bar{\alpha}y\rangle^{\prime},\qquad\forall x,y\in V^{\prime},\ \alpha\in E,

(107)

such that
(V,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle)
is isomorphic to
(V^{\prime},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle^{\prime})
at all finite places, and for
1\leq i\leq N
, the signature of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle^{\prime}
at the infinite place
\tau_{i}
of
F
is
(m_{i}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime})
. Let
G^{\prime}
be the similitude unitary group of
(V,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle^{\prime})
. We have

G^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R})=G\big(\prod_{i=1}^{N}U(m_{i}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime})\big),

(108)

and an isomorphism

G(\mdmathbb{A}_{f})\simeq G^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{A}_{f}).

(109)

Let
K^{\prime p}
be the compact open subgroup of
G^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{A}_{f}^{(p)})
corresponding to
K^{p}
under this isomorphism, and
K^{\prime}_{p}
be a hyperspecial subgroup at
p
. Let
X^{\prime}
be the special fiber of the Shimura variety parametrizing tuples
(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B})\in X^{\prime}(S)
, where

(1)
B
is an abelian scheme over
S
of dimension
nN
equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-action
\iota_{B}:\mathcal{O}_{E}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{End}_{S}B
, satisfying the Kottwitz signature condition given by
(m_{i}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime})
.

(2)
\lambda_{B}:B\to B^{\vee}
is a prime-to-
p
polarization, and the associated Rosati involution induces the complex conjugation on
\mathcal{O}_{E}
.

(3)
\eta_{B}
is an
K^{\prime p}
-level structure.

We denote by
\mathcal{B}/X^{\prime}
the universal abelian scheme. Let
P^{\prime}
be the Hodge parabolic of
G^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}\subseteq P^{\prime}
be a Borel subgroup. We consider an intermediate parabolic subgroup
B^{\prime}\subseteq P^{\prime}_{0}\subseteq P^{\prime}
such that the associated partial flag space
Y^{\prime}_{P^{\prime}_{0}}
over
X^{\prime}
is defined by specifying an additional vector subbundle

0\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}}\subseteq\omega_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}/X^{\prime},i}

(110)

of rank
m_{i}-r_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq N
. Let
Z^{\prime}\subseteq Y^{\prime}_{P_{0}^{\prime}}
denote the closed subscheme defined by the Schubert condition

V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i+1,(p)}_{m_{i+1}-r_{i+1}})\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}},\qquad\textnormal{for all $1\leq i\leq N$}.

(111)

We further define a moduli scheme
W
, such that
W(S)
parametrizes the equivalence classes of tuples
(A,\lambda,\eta,\allowbreak\iota,\allowbreak\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet},B,\allowbreak\lambda_{B},\allowbreak\eta_{B},\allowbreak\iota_{B},\allowbreak\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet},\phi)
, where

(1)
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet})\in Z(S)
;

(2)
(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet})\in Z^{\prime}(S)
;

(3)
\phi:B\to A
is an isogeny whose kernel is contained in
B[p]
, and the induced morphism
\phi_{\ast,i}
on de Rham homology groups satisfies

\textnormal{Ker }\phi_{\ast,i}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i+1,(p)}_{m_{i+1}-r_{i+1}}),\qquad\phi_{\ast,i}(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{i})=\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}},\qquad\textnormal{for all $1\leq i\leq N$.}

(112)

Here
\phi_{\ast,i}
stands for the induced morphism on
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
-components.

The forgetful maps induce the following diagram
W
Z
Z^{\prime}
Y_{P_{0}}
Y^{\prime}_{p_{0}^{\prime}}.
pr_{1}
pr_{2}
\supseteq
\subseteq

Next, we check the properties for
pr_{1}
and
pr_{2}
.

pr_{1}
is bijective on closed points:
It suffices to construct an inverse of
pr_{1}
on closed points. Given
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet})\in Z(k)
, by the condition of
\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet}
on
Z(k)
, the tuple
\{\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\}
forms an
F,V
-chain. By Theorem
4.2
, there exists a unique tuple
(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B})\in X^{\prime}(k)
and an isogeny
\phi:B\to A
. For each
i
, let
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}
be the inverse image of
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{i}/p\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
in
\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
. By the construction in the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we have
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}/p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}\simeq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/k)_{i}
. Let
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
denote the inverse image of
\omega_{i}\subseteq\tilde{D}(A)_{i}/p\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
in
\tilde{D}(A)_{i}
. Then the image of
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}/p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}
is a vector space of dimension
m_{i}-r_{i}
, and we denote it by
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}}
. Note that
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}}
is contained in
\omega_{B^{\vee}/k,i}
. Let

\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime i}:0\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}}\subseteq\omega_{B^{\vee}/k,i}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/k)_{i}

(113)

be a refinement of Hodge filtration for the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
-component, and let
\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet}=\{\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
. It is straightforward to check that
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet},B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet},\phi)
corresponds to a point in
W(k)
.

pr_{2}
is bijective on closed points:
We can construct the inverse of
pr_{2}
in a same manner as above, by considering the
F,V
-chain
\{V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i+1,(p)}_{m_{i+1}-r_{i+1}})\}_{i=1}^{N}
. We omit the remaining detailed arguments.

Next, we show the smoothness of
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
by computing their tangent bundles.

Smoothness of
Z
:
Let
\mdmathbb{I}=k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})
. Given a point
x\in Z(k)
corresponding to
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet})
, we must determine all possible liftings
x_{\mdmathbb{I}}\in Z(\mdmathbb{I})
of
x
. Recall the following theorem
[
19
, pp.116-118]
[
37
, Chapter V, Theorem 2.3]
[
36
]
.

Theorem 4.8

(Grothendieck-Messing-Serre-Tate)
.

Let
S
be a nilpotent thickening of a scheme
\overline{S}
with a divided-power structure. Let
AV_{S}
be the category of abelian schemes over
S^{\prime}
. Let
AV_{\overline{S}}^{+}
be the category of pairs
(\overline{A},\omega)
, where
\overline{A}
is an abelian scheme over
\overline{S}
, and
\omega\subseteq H_{1}^{\textnormal{cris}}(\overline{A}/\overline{S})_{S}
is a subbundle lifting
\omega_{\overline{A}^{\vee}/\overline{S}}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\overline{A}/\overline{S})
. Then the natural functor

\displaystyle AV_{S}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow AV^{+}_{\overline{S}}

(114)

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\mapsto(\overline{A}=A\times_{S}\overline{S},\omega_{A^{\vee}/S})

is an equivalence of categories.

Thus, to construct a lifting
A{I}
of
A
, it suffices to construct a subbundle
\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I}}
that lifts
\omega_{A^{\vee}/k}
. To lift the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-action, we must take
\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}(\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I},\tilde{\tau}_{i}}\oplus\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I},\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}})
such that the lifting is componentwise. To lift the polarization
\lambda
, the lifting must satisfy
\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I},\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}}=\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I},\tilde{\tau}_{i}}^{\perp}
under the pairing induced by
\lambda
. To lift
\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet}
, it suffices to choose subbundles
0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i},\mdmathbb{I}}\subseteq\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I},\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
lifting
\mathcal{E}_{r_{i}}^{i}
for each
i
, such that
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i},\mdmathbb{I}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1},\mdmathbb{I}})
for all
1\leq i\leq N
.

Note that the
p
-th power map on
k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})
takes
\epsilon
to 0. Thus,
V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1},\mdmathbb{I}})
is independent of the choice of lifting
\mathcal{E}^{i+1}_{r_{i+1}}
. In other words, for each
i
, it suffices to first lift
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}
to a sub-bundle of the pre-fixed bundle
V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1}})
of rank
r_{i}
, and then lift
\omega_{A^{\vee}/k,i}
to a vector bundle containing
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}
and contained in
H^{\textnormal{cris}}_{1}(A/K)_{\mdmathbb{I}}
. We obtain

0\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{H}om\big(\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}},V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1}})/\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}_{Z/k}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{H}om(\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}/Z,i}/\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}},H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/Z)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}/\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}/Z,i}\big)\longrightarrow 0.

(115)

This calculation of the tangent bundle
\mathcal{T}_{Z/k}
demonstrates that
Z
is smooth.

Smoothness of
Z^{\prime}
:
The analysis of
\mathcal{T}_{Z^{\prime}/k}
is analogous.

pr_{2}
is a bijection on tangent spaces:
Let
y=(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil}^{\bullet},B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet},\phi)\in W(k)
be the inverse image of
x
. Given a lift
x_{\mdmathbb{I}}
of
x
, it suffices to construct a point
y_{\mdmathbb{I}}\in W(\mdmathbb{I})
lifting
y
. Let
\psi:A\to B
be the dual isogeny of
\phi
. Since
\phi\circ\psi=[p]_{A}
and
\psi\circ\phi=[p]_{B}
, we have

\textnormal{Im }{}_{\ast,i}=\textnormal{Ker }\phi_{\ast,i},\qquad\textnormal{Ker }{}_{\ast,i}=\textnormal{Im }\phi_{\ast,i}.

(116)

Here, by slight abuse of notation, we use
\phi_{\ast,i},{}_{\ast,i}
to denote the induced morphisms on the crystalline homology groups. We take the lifting
\omega_{A^{\vee}_{\mdmathbb{I}}/\mdmathbb{I}}
to be
{}_{\ast,i}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m_{i}-r_{i}})
and
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i},\mdmathbb{I}}
to be
\textnormal{Im }\phi_{\ast,i}
. Applying Theorem
4.8
, we obtain
(A{I},\lambda{I},\eta_{\mdmathbb{I}},\iota_{\mdmathbb{I}},\textnormal{Fil}^{\prime\bullet}_{\mdmathbb{I}})
and
\phi_{\mdmathbb{I}}:B_{\mdmathbb{I}}\to A_{\mdmathbb{I}}
. Combining these data gives a point
y_{\mdmathbb{I}}
. This construction gives the inverse of
pr_{2}
.

Remark 4.9
.

The map
pr_{1}
does not induce an isomorphism on tangent spaces due to Frobenius twists issues. However, we know that
pr_{2}
is an isomorphism because it induces a bijection on both closed points and tangent spaces. Therefore,
W
is smooth, and by Lemma
4.5
, we deduce that these two strata
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
in partial flag spaces of different Shimura varieties are isomorphism up to Frobenius twists. This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

Remark 4.10
.

There is a dual version of this proposition: Notations and assumptions as above, except that for each
1\leq i\leq N
,
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{s_{i}}
is a sub-bundle of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)
of rank
s_{i}
containing
\omega_{i}
. Then
Z
is, up to some Frobenius twist, isomorphic to a stratum
Z^{\prime\prime}
in a partial flag space
Y^{\prime\prime}_{P_{0}^{\prime\prime}}
over
X^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime\prime}
is the special fiber of a unitary Shimura variety with hyperspecial level structure and signature
(m_{i}^{\prime\prime},n_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i=1}^{N}
, where

m_{i}^{\prime\prime}=m_{i}-s_{i}+s_{i-1},\quad n_{i}^{\prime\prime}=n-m_{i}^{\prime\prime},\quad\textnormal{for $1\leq i\leq N$}.

(117)

The proof is analogous.

This proposition has the limitation that if the signature at some
\tau_{i}
is
(0,n)
, then the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}
can only be
0
. Therefore, we strengthen the proposition by introducing essential Frobenius and Verschiebung maps.

Definition 4.11
.

We define the essential Frobenius map
F_{es}:H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}^{(p)}/X)_{i+1}\to H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}
and the essential Verschiebung map
V_{es}:H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}\to H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}^{(p)}/X)_{i+1}
to be

F_{es}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &F,\qquad\quad\textnormal{if $m_{i+1}\neq n$},\\
&V^{-1},\qquad\textnormal{if $m_{i+1}=n$}.\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad V_{es}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &V,\qquad\quad\textnormal{if $m_{i+1}\neq 0$},\\
&F^{-1},\qquad\textnormal{if $m_{i+1}=0$}.\end{aligned}\right.

(118)

We define
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
as the image of
V_{es}
. More precisely,
\tilde{\omega}_{i}=\omega_{i}
if
m_{i}\neq 0
, and
\tilde{\omega}_{i}=H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}
otherwise. Let
\tilde{m}_{i}
denote the rank of
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
and
\tilde{n}_{i}=n-\tilde{m}_{i}
. We define an
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain in a similar manner as before.

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
B\subseteq P_{0}\subseteq P
be an intermediate parabolic subgroup such that the associated partial flag space
Y_{P_{0}}
is defined by recording at most one subbundle
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\subseteq\tilde{\omega}_{i}
of rank
r_{i}
for each
i
(
r_{i}
can be
m_{i}
, in which case we do not record anything at the
\tau_{i}
-component). Let
Z
be the closed subscheme of
Y_{P_{0}}
defined by the condition

\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}\subseteq V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1}})

(119)

for all
i
. If moreover
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{E}^{i+1,(p)}_{r_{i+1}})
whenever
m_{i}=0
. Then
Z
is, up to some Frobenius factor, isomorphic to a stratum
Z^{\prime}
in some partial flag space
Y^{\prime}_{P_{0}^{\prime}}
over
X^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime}
is the special fiber of a unitary Shimura variety with hyperspecial level structure and signature condition

m_{i}^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\textnormal{if $m_{i-1}=0$},\\
&n-r_{i}+r_{i-1},\qquad\textnormal{ if $m_{i}=0$ and $m_{i-1}\neq 0$},\\
&m_{i}-r_{i}+r_{i-1},\quad\ \ \textnormal{ otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(120)

Proof:
Pointwise, let
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}_{r_{i}}
be the inverse images of
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{r_{i}}
in
\tilde{D}(A)
via
\tilde{D}(A)/p\tilde{D}(A)\simeq D(A)
. We define
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime i}_{r_{i}}
as

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime i}_{r_{i}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}_{r_{i}},\qquad\ \textnormal{ if $m_{i}\neq 0$},\\
&p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}_{r_{i}},\qquad\textnormal{if $m_{i}=0$}.\end{aligned}\right.

(121)

However, the set
\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime i}_{r_{i}}\}
, is not an
F,V
-chain, and we cannot directly construct the universal
p
-isogeny
\phi:B\to A
using the method in the proof of Proposition
4.2
, since
p\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}_{r_{i}}\subseteq pD(A)_{i}
. However, we can consider the
F,V
-chain
\{\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{i}\}
given by

\tilde{J}_{i}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{i}_{r_{i}},\qquad\quad\textnormal{ if $m_{i}\neq 0$},\\
&p\tilde{D}(A),\qquad\textnormal{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(122)

Thus,
\tilde{J}_{i}
forms an
F,V
-chain in
\tilde{D}(A)
, and
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime i}_{r_{i}}
forms an
F,V
-chain in
\oplus_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{J}_{i}
. Applying the construction in the proof of Proposition
4.2
twice, one can construct universal abelian schemes
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}
and universal
p
-isogenies

\phi_{1}:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A},\qquad\phi_{2}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}

(123)

together with the data of auxiliary vector subbundles (plus inclusion relations). In this manner, we establish the desired isomorphism (up to Frobenius twists).
\hfill\square

This refined version will be frequently used in our later proof of the sufficiency part of the ampleness criterion.

4.3 Review of some results on positivity in algebraic geometry.

We will frequently use the following list of results in
[
34
]
on positivity in algebraic geometry.

Definition 4.13
.

[
34
, Definition 1.4.1]
Let
X
be a complete variety. A line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
is called
numerically effective
if

\big(\mathcal{L}\cdot C\big)\geq 0

(124)

for every irreducible curve
C\subseteq X
.

Proposition 4.14
.

[
34
, Proposition 1.2.13]

Let
f:X\to Y
be a finite morphism of proper schemes and let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle over
Y
. Then
f^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
is ample if
\mathcal{L}
is ample. Furthermore, if
f
is surjective, then the converse holds.

Proposition 4.15
.

[
34
, Example 1.4.4]

Let
f:X\to Y
be a proper morphism of proper schemes, and
\mathcal{L}
a line bundle over
Y
. Then
f^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
is nef if
\mathcal{L}
is nef. Furthermore, if
f
is surjective, then the converse holds.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme and
D_{1},D_{2}\in\textnormal{Pic $X$}_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. If
D_{1}
is ample and
D_{2}
is nef, then
D_{1}+D_{2}
is ample.

Definition 4.17
.

[
34
, Definition 1.7.1]

Let
f:X\to T
be a proper morphism of algebraic varieties and
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
X
.

(1)
\mathcal{L}
is very ample relative to
f
, or
f
-very ample if the canonical map

\rho:f^{\ast}f_{\ast}\mathcal{L}\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}

(125)

is surjective and defines an embedding
X\hookrightarrow\mdmathbb{P}(f_{\ast}\mathcal{L})
of schemes over
T
.

(2)
\mathcal{L}
is ample relative to
f
, or
f
-ample, if
f^{\otimes m}
is
f
-very ample for some
m>0
.

Proposition 4.18
.

[
34
, Theorem 1.7.8]
Let
f
be a morphism of proper noetherian schemes. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
X
. For
t\in T
set

X_{t}=f^{-1}(t),\qquad\mathcal{L}_{t}=\mathcal{L}|_{X_{t}}.

(126)

Then
\mathcal{L}
is
f
-ample if and only if
\mathcal{L}_{t}
is ample on
X_{t}
for all
t\in T
(That is, fiberwise ample).

Proposition 4.19
.

[
34
, Proposition 1.7.10]

Let
f:X\to T
be a morphism of projective schemes. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
X
, and
\mathcal{E}
be an ample line bundle on
T
. Then
\mathcal{L}
is
f
-ample if and only if
\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\otimes m}
is an ample line bundle on
X
for all
m\gg 0.

5  Proof of the ampleness criterion: The necessity part

In this section, we assume
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is an ample line bundle on the flag space
Y
. We will show that the weight
\lambda
must satisfy the inequalities in Theorem
3.24
, Theorem
3.25
, Theorem
3.26
, and Theorem
3.27
, depending on the signature condition of the underlying Shimura variety. Recall that
T=\{i|\ m_{i}\neq 0,n\}
denotes the
essential set
of
X
, and its cardinality
t=\#T
is the
essential degree
of
X
.

The fibers of the projection
Y\to X
are flag varieties, so the ampleness implies that the weight is regular and
L
-dominant. The remaining inequalities come from a case-by-case analysis. The basic idea is to restrict
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
to "special" subvarieties.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a vector bundle over a base scheme
S
. Throughout this section, we write
[\mathcal{V}]
for the class of
\textnormal{det }\mathcal{V}
in
(\textnormal{Pic }S)_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
.

Remark 5.1
.

By Proposition
4.14
,
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is ample if and only if its pullback under finite surjective morphisms is ample. So the Frobenius twists in Proposition
4.2
and
4.12
may be ignored for our purpose.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any vector bundle
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\omega_{i}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}\supseteq\omega_{i}
) on
X
, we have

[V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{(p)})]=p[\mathcal{F}]-p[\omega_{i}]\qquad(\textnormal{resp. }F(\mathcal{E}^{(p)})=p[\mathcal{E}]-p[{\omega}_{i}])

(127)

in
(\textnormal{Pic }X)_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. Moreover, this holds for their pullbacks via any proper morphism
Z\to X
.

Proof:
Since
[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i-1}]=0
, the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\textnormal{Ker }V_{i-1}\longrightarrow H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i-1}\longrightarrow\omega_{i}^{(p)}\longrightarrow 0

(128)

implies that
[\textnormal{Ker $V_{i-1}$}]=-p[\omega_{i}]
in
(\textnormal{Pic $X$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. The short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\textnormal{Ker $V_{i-1}$}\longrightarrow V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{(p)})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{(p)}\longrightarrow 0

(129)

yields
[V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{(p)})]=[\mathcal{F}^{(p)}]+[\textnormal{Ker }V_{i-1}]\allowbreak=p[\mathcal{F}]-p[\omega_{i}]
. The formula for
\mathcal{E}
can be deduced similarly.
\hfill\square

We first deal with the case when
X
has essential degree 1.

Lemma 5.3
.

If
t=1
, we assume
T=\{i_{1}\}=\{1\}
without loss of generality. If moreover
m_{1}=n-1
and

[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)]=[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\{k^{i}_{j}\})]=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]

is ample, then
\lambda
is
L
-regular dominant and

\bigg(\sum_{u=0}^{n-2}p^{Nu}\bigg)\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}(k^{1+j}_{1}-k^{1+j}_{n})>\sum_{r=2}^{n-1}\bigg(p^{(r-2)N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}(k^{1+j}_{r}-k^{1+j}_{n})\bigg).

(130)

Note that when
n=2
the right hand side is 0.

Proof:
By definition, the vector bundle
\omega_{1}=\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}/X,\tilde{\tau}_{1}}
is locally free of rank
1
, and the vector bundle
\omega_{i}=\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}/X,\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
has rank
0
or
n
for
2\leq i\leq N
. This signature condition implies that the map

V_{es}:H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}\longrightarrow H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i+1}

(131)

is an isomorphism for
1\leq i\leq N-1
, and has kernel locally free of rank
1
for
i=N
.

Let
Z\subseteq Y
be the closed subscheme defined by the condition

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{j}^{i+1,(p)}),\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq i\leq N-1,1\leq j<n$},

(132)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{N}_{j}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{j-1}^{1,(p)}),\qquad\ \textnormal{for $2\leq j<n$}.

This condition corresponds to (the closure of) a one dimensional stratum on the flag space. By Lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{i+1}_{j}],\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq i\leq N-1,1\leq j<n$},

(133)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{N}_{j}]=p([\mathcal{F}^{1}_{j-1}]-[\omega_{1}]),\qquad\textnormal{for $2\leq j<n$}.

in
(\textnormal{Pic $Z$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. We can express each class in terms of
[\omega_{1}]
:

[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}]=\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\big(1+\frac{1}{p^{N}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p^{(n-j-1)N}}\big)[\omega_{1}],\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq i\leq N,1\leq j<n$}.

(134)

The class
[\omega_{1}]
is nef on
Z
, since it is the pullback of the class of the Hodge line bundle
\textnormal{det }\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\vee}/X}
on
X
. We claim that
[\omega_{1}]
is, moreover, ample. Notice that the image of
Z
under the projection map
Y\to X
is the closure of the unique 1-dimensional Ekedahl–Oort stratum (One could use Example
3.11
to write down the conditions), and this projection map onto its image is actually a Frobenius factor. Thus, the pullback of the Hodge bundle to
Z
is ample. There is an alternative way to show this: We construct a strata Hasse invariant by

\mathcal{F}^{N}_{1}\longrightarrow V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{1})/\textnormal{Ker }V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle V}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{1}.

(135)

Here, the first map is induced by the inclusion map. This gives a section
h\in H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{N,-1}_{1})
whose zero locus is the zero dimensional Ekedahl–Oort stratum on
Y
. By the above computation of classes, we have

[h]=p(1-\frac{1}{p^{N(n-1)}})[\omega_{1}].

(136)

This shows that
[\omega_{1}]
has positive degree on
Z
, hence ample.

Plugging the above equations into
[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)]|_{Z}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|_{Z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]\big|_{Z}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]\big|_{Z}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}k^{i}_{n}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{n-1}]

(137)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(k^{i}_{j}-k^{i}_{n})[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]\big|_{Z}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\sum_{j=2}^{n-1}(-\frac{1}{p^{(n-j)N}})(k^{i}_{j}-k^{i}_{n})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\big(1+\frac{1}{p^{N}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p^{(n-2)N}}\big)(k^{i}_{1}-k^{i}_{n})\bigg)[\omega_{1}]\big|_{Z}.

The positivity of the coefficient gives the desired inequality.
\hfill\square

Lemma 5.4
.

If
t=\#T\geq 2
and
[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)]
is ample, then
\lambda
is
L
-regular dominant and

p^{a(i_{l})}\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}(k_{1}^{i_{l}+j}-k_{n}^{i_{l}+j})>k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}-k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+1},\qquad\textnormal{for all $1\leq l\leq t$}.

(138)

Here, the subscripts of
i_{l}
are viewed as modulo
t
.

Proof:
Let
Z
be the stratum over which the Hodge filtration agrees with the conjugate filtration at
\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{i_{l}+1},\dots,\tau_{i_{l+1}-1}
-components, i.e.,
Z
is the closed subscheme defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{r}=V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j+1,(p)}_{r}),\qquad\textnormal{for $0\leq j<a(i_{l})-1,1\leq r<n$},

(139)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{r}=F_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+r}^{i_{l+1},(p)}),\qquad\ \textnormal{for $1\leq r\leq n_{i_{l+1}}$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{r}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{r-n_{i_{l+1}}}^{i_{l+1},(p)}),\qquad\textnormal{for $n_{i_{l+1}}<r<n$}.

In fact, we may write the last two conditions uniformly by viewing the subscripts as modulo
n
. By Lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{r}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j+1}_{r}],\qquad\textnormal{for $0\leq j<a(i_{l})-1,1\leq r<n$},

(140)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{r}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+r}/\omega_{i_{l+1}}],\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq r<n$},

in
(\textnormal{Pic $Z$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. Assume
m_{i_{l}}+m_{i_{l+1}}\geq n
for simplicity, the above condition implies the tuple

(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{1},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l}-1},\omega_{i_{l}},\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+1}_{m_{i_{l}}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{m_{i_{l}}},H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l+1}},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{N})

(141)

forms an
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain. By Proposition
4.12
, we obtain a correspondence

W
Z
Z^{\prime}
Y
Z^{\prime\prime}
X^{\prime}
pr_{1}
pr_{2}
\supseteq
\pi
\pi

To make the proof more readable, we recall the moduli problems of all the auxiliary varieties in this diagram.

Here,
X^{\prime}
is a unitary Shimura variety with signature
(m^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. We note that
m^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}=m_{i_{l}}+m_{i_{l+1}}-n
. Since
Z
involves the data of a refinement of Hodge filtration, we must record the data for the auxiliary varieties correspondingly. More precisely,
Z^{\prime}
is the moduli space whose
S
points are tuples
(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}_{B})
where

\bullet

(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B})\in X^{\prime}(S)
.

\bullet

\textnormal{Fil}_{B}
is a refinement of the Hodge filtration, which agrees with
Fil
away from the
i_{l},\dots,i_{l+1}
-places, and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\textnormal{Fil}_{B}^{i_{l}+j}\textnormal{ is the Hodge filtration for $0\leq j<a(i_{l})$, i.e., we do not take any refinements},\\
&\textnormal{Fil}_{B}^{i_{l+1}}:0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}-1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}}=\omega_{B,i_{l+1}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}+1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{n-1}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{i_{l+1}}.\end{aligned}

(142)

We take
W
to be the moduli space whose
S
-points consist of tuples
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil},B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}_{B},\varphi)
, where

\bullet

(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\textnormal{Fil})\in Z(S)
.

\bullet

(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}_{B})\in Z^{\prime}(S)
.

\bullet

\varphi:B\to A
is an isogeny whose kernel is killed by
p
, such that the induced morphism
\varphi_{\ast}:\allowbreak H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}({B}/S)\allowbreak\to H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}({A}/S)
is an isomorphism away from the
\tau_{i_{l}},\tau_{i_{l}+1},\dots,\tau_{i_{l+1}}
-components, and

\varphi_{i,\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{i}_{j})=\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j},\qquad\textnormal{for $i\notin\{i_{l},\dots,i_{l+1}\}$ and $1\leq j<n$}.

(143)

At
\tau_{i_{l}},\dots,\tau_{i_{l+1}-1}
, we require

\displaystyle\textnormal{Im }\varphi_{i_{l+1}-j,\ast}=\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-j}_{m_{i_{l}}},\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq j\leq a(i_{l})$}.

(144)

At
\tau_{i_{l+1}}
, we require
\varphi_{i_{l+1},\ast}
to be an isomorphism, and

\varphi_{i_{l+1},\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{r})=\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{r},\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq r<n$}.

(145)

We define
Z^{\prime\prime}
as the partial flag space over
X^{\prime}
which only records refinement of the Hodge filtration at places away from the
\tau_{i_{l}},\dots,\tau_{i_{l+1}}
-components. The forgetful map
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
is proper surjective, whose fibers are isomorphic to flag varieties

Fl_{GL_{2n-m_{i_{l}}-m_{i_{l+1}}}}\times Fl_{GL_{m_{i_{l}}+m_{i_{l+1}}-n}}.

(146)

It follows from Proposition
4.6
(with additional filtration data) that
pr_{1}
and
pr_{2}
are isomorphisms up to Frobenius factors. If
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is ample, it has to be ample on
Z
, hence
Z^{\prime}
, hence the fibers of
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
. Pick any closed point
z^{\prime\prime}\in Z^{\prime\prime}
, let
C_{z^{\prime\prime}}
denote the fiber of
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
at
z^{\prime\prime}
. We have

\displaystyle\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{u=1}^{n}k^{i}_{u}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{u}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{u-1}]|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

(147)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})}\sum_{u=1}^{n}k^{i_{l}+j}_{u}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{u}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{u-1}]\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}\sum_{u=1}^{n}k^{i_{l}+j}_{u}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{u}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+j}_{u-1}]+\sum_{v=1}^{n}k^{i_{l+1}}_{v}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v-1}]\bigg)\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{u=1}^{n}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j-1}k_{u}^{i_{l}+j}\big)[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{u}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{u-1}]+\sum_{v=1}^{n}k^{i_{l+1}}_{v}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v-1}]\bigg)\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{u=1}^{n_{i_{l+1}}}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k_{u}^{i_{l}+j}\big)[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+u}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+u-1}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{u=n_{i_{l+1}}+1}^{n}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k_{u}^{i_{l}+j}\big)[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{u-n_{i_{l+1}}}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{u-n_{i_{l+1}}-1}]+\sum_{v=1}^{n}k^{i_{l+1}}_{v}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}}_{v-1}]\big)\bigg)\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{u=1}^{n_{i_{l+1}}}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k_{u}^{i_{l}+j}\big)[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+u}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+u-1}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{u=n_{i_{l+1}}+1}^{n}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k_{u}^{i_{l}+j}\big)[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{u-n_{i_{l+1}}}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{u-n_{i_{l+1}}-1}]+\sum_{v=1}^{n}k^{i_{l+1}}_{v}[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{v}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{v-1}]\big)\bigg)\big|_{C_{z^{\prime\prime}}}.

Over each fiber
C_{z^{\prime\prime}}
, the weight is required to be regular dominant. This in particular implies that "the coefficient of
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+1}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}]
" is bigger than "the coefficient of
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}-1}]
". That is,

\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k^{i_{l}+j}_{1}\big)+k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+1}>\big(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}p^{a(i_{l})-j}k^{i_{l}+j}_{n}\big)+k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}.

(148)

In other words,

p^{a(i_{l})}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{a(i_{l})-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}\big(k^{i_{l}+j}_{1}-k^{i_{l}+j}_{n}\big)\bigg)>k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}-k^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+1}.

(149)

We remark that the other inequalities derived from the regular dominant condition are redundant.

If we are in the case
m_{i_{l}}+m_{i_{l+1}}<n
, we can consider the
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain

(0,\dots,0,\omega_{i_{l}},\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}+1}_{m_{i_{l}}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{i_{l+1}-1}_{m_{i_{l}}},0,\dots,0),

(150)

and apply Proposition
4.12
to show that
Z
is, up to Frobenius factors, isomorphic to a stratum
Z^{\prime}
in the partial flag space of an auxiliary unitary Shimura variety with different signature. One can construct a projection
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
whose fibers are isomorphic to

Fl_{GL_{n-m_{i_{l}}-m_{i_{l+1}}}}\times Fl_{GL_{m_{i_{l}}+m_{i_{l+1}}}}

(151)

in a completely same manner. Restricting to geometric fibers of this morphism, the regular dominant condition again implies "the coefficient of
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}+1}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}]
" is bigger than "the coefficient of
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}/\mathcal{E}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}-1}]
". A direct but boring computation of Picard group relations yields the same inequality. An alternative approach is to work on the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}
-side and reduce to the previous case.

Letting
l
run over
1,2,\dots,t
, we obtain the desired necessary condition.
\hfill\square

Lemma 5.5
.

If
t=1
, we assume
T=\{i_{1}\}=\{1\}
for simplicity. If moreover
m_{1}\notin\{1,n-1\}
and

[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)]=[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\{k^{i}_{j}\})]=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]

(152)

is ample, then
\lambda
is
L
-regular dominant and

p^{N}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}(k^{1+j}_{1}-k^{1+j}_{n})\big)>k^{1}_{m_{1}}-k^{1}_{m_{1}+1}.

(153)

Remark 5.6
.

The inequalities in Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.5
could be written uniformly. However, since there is only one essential place in this case, the choice of the
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain in the proof of Lemma
5.4
is not valid in this case. It is somewhat surprising that the criterion arises from a relatively unusual construction.

Proof:
Without loss of generality, we may assume
m_{2}=n
, otherwise, we may consider the filtrations on
\tau^{c}
-side. This is a minor technical assumption to ensure that the
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain in the following is a genuine
F,V
-chain. Let
Z
denote the stratum defined by the condition

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{i+1,(p)}_{j}),\qquad

\displaystyle\textnormal{for $2\leq i\leq N-1,1\leq j\leq n$},

(154)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{1}_{j}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{j}),\qquad

\displaystyle\textnormal{for $j\neq m_{1}$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{N}_{j}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{j-n_{1}}),\qquad

\displaystyle\textnormal{for $j\notin\{n_{1},m_{1}\}$}.

Here, the subscripts are again treated modulo
n
. The stratum
Z
is 2-dimensional if
m_{1}=n_{1}
, and is 3-dimensional if
m_{1}\neq n_{1}
. Note that over
Z
, the tuple

(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m_{1}-1},\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{1}})

(155)

forms an
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain. It is indeed an
F,V
-chain by assumption. Applying Proposition
4.2
, we obtain a correspondence

W
Z
Z^{\prime}
Y
Z^{\prime\prime}
X^{\prime}
pr_{1}
pr_{2}
\supseteq
\pi
\pi

For simplicity, we use the same letters as in the previous lemma, but they stand for entirely different objects. We now recall their moduli problems:

X^{\prime}
is the auxiliary Shimura variety of appropriate level structure, as given by Proposition
4.6
. The signature of
X^{\prime}
changes only at the first two places, with
m_{1}^{\prime}=m_{1}+1
and
m_{2}=n-1
.

Y^{\prime}
is the full flag space over
X^{\prime}
, which means that
Y^{\prime}(S)
consists of isomorphism classes of tuples
(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\textnormal{Fil}_{B})
, where

\bullet

(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B})\in X^{\prime}(S)
;

\bullet

\textnormal{Fil}_{B}
is a refinement of the Hodge filtration:

\displaystyle\textnormal{Fil}_{B}^{1}:0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{m_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{m_{1}+1}=\omega_{B,1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{m_{1}+2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n-1}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{1},

(156)

\displaystyle\textnormal{Fil}_{B}^{2}:0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{2}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{2}_{n-2}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{2}_{n-1}=\omega_{B,2}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{2},

\displaystyle\textnormal{Fil}_{B}^{1+j}:0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1+j}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1+j}_{n-1}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{1+j},\qquad\textnormal{for $2\leq j\leq N-1$}.

Z^{\prime}\subseteq Y^{\prime}
is the stratum defined by the condition (In the following, we always assume
m_{1}\geq n_{1}
for simplicity. Otherwise, one changes the third equality to be "for
u\geq n_{1}
" and the fourth equality to be "for
u\leq n_{1}-1
and
u\neq n_{1}-m_{1}
".)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{1}_{u}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{2,(p)}_{u-1}),\quad\ \ \ \qquad\textnormal{for $u\notin\{1,m_{1}+1\}$},

(157)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{1+j}_{u}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{E}^{2+j,(p)}_{u}),\qquad\textnormal{for $1\leq j\leq N-2,1\leq u\leq n-1$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{N}_{u}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{1,(p)}_{u-n_{1}+1}),\ \ \qquad\textnormal{for $u\geq n_{1}$ and $u\neq 2n_{1}$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{N}_{u}=F(\mathcal{E}^{1,(p)}_{u+m_{1}+1}),\ \quad\qquad\textnormal{for $u\leq n_{1}-1$}.

We remark that if
m_{1}=n_{1}
, then the third condition above becomes "for
u\geq n_{1}
". This again justifies the fact that
Z
is 3-dimensional if
m_{1}\neq n_{1}
, and 2-dimensional if
m_{1}=n_{1}
.

W
is the moduli space whose
S
points consists of tuples
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B},\varphi)
, where

\bullet

(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota)\in Z(S)
;

\bullet

(B,\lambda_{B},\eta_{B},\iota_{B})\in Z^{\prime}(S)
;

\bullet

\varphi:B\to A
is an isogeny, whose kernel is killed by
p
, and the induced morphism
\varphi_{i,\ast}:\allowbreak H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/S)_{i}\allowbreak\to H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/S)_{i}
satisfies (let
\psi
be the dual isogeny of
\varphi
)

\displaystyle\textnormal{At $\tau_{1}$:}

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ker }\varphi_{1,\ast}=\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1},

(158)

\displaystyle\varphi_{1,\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{1}_{u})=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{u-n_{1}-1},\ \qquad\textnormal{for $n_{1}+2\leq u\leq n$ and $u\neq m_{1}+1$},

\displaystyle{}_{1,\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{v})=\mathcal{E}^{1}_{v-m_{1}+1},\qquad\textnormal{for $m_{1}\leq v\leq n-1$}.

\displaystyle\textnormal{At $\tau_{j}$ for $2\leq j\leq N$:}

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ker }\varphi_{j,\ast}=\mathcal{E}^{j}_{n_{1}},

\displaystyle\varphi_{j,\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{j}_{u})=\mathcal{F}^{j}_{u-n_{1}},\qquad\ \textnormal{for $n_{1}+1\leq u\leq n$},

\displaystyle{}_{j,\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{j}_{v})=\mathcal{E}^{j}_{v-m_{1}},\qquad\textnormal{for $m_{1}+1\leq v\leq n-1$}.

We remark that if
m_{1}<n_{1}
, we change the condition at
\tau_{1}
to be

\displaystyle\textnormal{At $\tau_{1}$:}

\displaystyle\textnormal{Ker }\varphi_{1,\ast}=\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1},

(159)

\displaystyle\varphi_{1,\ast}(\mathcal{E}^{1}_{u})=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{u-n_{1}-1},\ \qquad\textnormal{for $n_{1}+2\leq u\leq n$},

\displaystyle{}_{1,\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{v})=\mathcal{E}^{1}_{v-m_{1}+1},\qquad\textnormal{for $m_{1}\leq v\leq n-1$ and $v\neq 2m_{1}$}.

Y^{\prime\prime}
is a partial flag space over
X^{\prime}
which records all the
\mathcal{E}^{i}_{j}
’s except that

\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1},\qquad\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1},\qquad\textnormal{and }\mathcal{E}^{j}_{n_{1}},\textnormal{ for $2\leq j\leq N$}.

(160)

Z^{\prime\prime}
is the image of
Z^{\prime}
under the natural forgetful map
Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
. In fact,
Z
is the stratum cut out by the same conditions as
Z^{\prime}
except for those involving
\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1}
, and
\mathcal{E}^{j}_{n_{1}}
for
2\leq j\leq N
. Giving a point on the fiber of
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
is equivalent to choosing
0\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{1}_{2}
and putting

\mathcal{E}^{N}_{n_{1}}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{1,(p)}_{1}),\ \mathcal{E}^{N-1}_{n_{1}}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{E}^{N,(p)}_{n_{1}}),\ \dots,\mathcal{E}^{2}_{n_{1}}=V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{E}^{3,(p)}_{n_{1}}),\ \mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{E}^{2,(p)}_{n_{1}}).

(161)

Therefore, the fibers are
\mdmathbb{P}^{1}
, and the above vector bundles are the only nontrivial classes on the fibers.

If
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is ample, then it is ample on
Z^{\prime}
, and consequently, ample over the fibers of
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}
. Let
z\in Z^{\prime\prime}(k)
be a closed point, then in
Pic
\mdmathbb{P}^{1}_{z}
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m_{1}+1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m_{1}}]=[\mathcal{E}^{1}_{2}/\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}]=[\mathcal{O}(1)],

(162)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{1}+1}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{1}}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{3}_{m_{1}+1}/\mathcal{F}^{3}_{m_{1}}]=\cdots=p^{N-2}[\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{1}+1}/\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{1}}]=p^{N-2}[\mathcal{E}^{N}_{1}]=0,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m_{1}-1}]=[\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}]=[\mathcal{O}(-1)],

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{1}-1}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{3}_{m_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{3}_{m_{1}-1}]=\cdots=p^{N-2}[\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{1}-1}]=[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(B/X^{\prime})_{2}/\omega_{B,2}]=0,

\displaystyle[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-1}]=p[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{2}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{n-1}]=p^{2}[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{3}/\mathcal{F}^{3}_{n-1}]=\cdots=p^{N-1}[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{N}/\mathcal{F}^{N}_{n-1}]

\displaystyle[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-1}]=[\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}+1}/\mathcal{E}^{1}_{n_{1}}]=\cdots=p^{N-1}[\mathcal{E}^{N}_{n_{1}}/\mathcal{E}^{N}_{n_{1}-1}]=p^{N}[\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}]=p^{N}[\mathcal{O}(-1)],

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}]=p[\mathcal{F}^{2}_{1}]=\cdots=p^{N-1}[\mathcal{F}^{N}_{1}],

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}^{N}_{1}]=[\mathcal{E}^{N}_{n_{1}+1}/\mathcal{E}^{N}_{n_{1}}]=p[\mathcal{E}^{1}_{2}/\mathcal{E}^{1}_{1}]=p[\mathcal{O}(1)].

For
[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]
, where
(i,j)
are not in the above list, it is trivial on the fiber. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\big|_{\mdmathbb{P}^{1}_{z}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{n}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]\big|_{\mdmathbb{P}^{1}_{z}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\in\{1,m_{1},m_{1}+1,n\}}k^{i}_{j}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j-1}]\big|_{\mdmathbb{P}^{1}_{z}}

(163)

\displaystyle=\big(k^{1}_{m_{1}+1}-k^{1}_{m_{1}}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{N+1-i}k^{i}_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{N+1-i}k^{i}_{1}\big)[\mathcal{O}(1)].

This gives the desired inequality

p^{N}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{p^{j}}(k^{1+j}_{1}-k^{1+j}_{n})\big)>k^{1}_{m_{1}}-k^{1}_{m_{1}+1}.

(164)

This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

Combining these lemmas, we have established the necessity part of the ampleness criterion by a case-by-case argument.

Remark 5.7
.

The proofs of the lemmas in this section remain valid if we replace "ample" by "nef", "regular dominant" by "dominant", and
>
by
\geq
. Therefore, we also obtained the necessity part of the
nefness
criterion.

Remark 5.8
.

A line bundle over a projective scheme is nef if and only if its pullback along a proper surjective morphism is nef. Applying this to the projection
Y=Y_{B}\to Y_{P^{\prime}}
, we have also established the necessity part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{P^{\prime}}(\lambda)
. Here,
B\subseteq P^{\prime}\subseteq P
is an intermediate parabolic subgroup, and
\lambda
is a character of
P^{\prime}
.

6  Slopes

The goal of this section is to introduce the notion that we call "slope" to measure different Ekedahl–Oort strata (on Shimura variety or any partial flag space). The proof of the sufficiency part of the ampleness (or nefness) criterion in the next chapter is based on induction with respect to slopes.

6.1 Setup and definitions.

In the following, we continue to denote by
X
the special fiber of the unitary Shimura variety with good reduction, where we have the vector bundles
\omega_{i}
. Let
\pi:T\to X
be any proper morphism. As before, we modify the Frobenius and Verschiebung morphisms to be
F_{es}
and
V_{es}
, respectively. Let
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
be the image of
V_{es}
, i.e.,

\tilde{\omega}_{i}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\omega_{i},\qquad\qquad\quad\ \ \textnormal{if $m_{i}\neq 0$},\\
&H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i},\qquad\textnormal{if $m_{i}=0$}.\end{aligned}\right.

(165)

Let
\tilde{m}_{i}
denote the rank of
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
and
\tilde{n}_{i}=n-\tilde{m}_{i}
. By abuse of notation, for any vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
on
X
, we denote the pullback
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{V}
to
T
still by
\mathcal{V}
as long as the underlying scheme we are considering is obvious in the context.

For
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
two subbundles of a vector bundle
\mathcal{M}
, the intersection sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{1}\cap\mathcal{M}_{2}
is in general not a coherent sheaf, but the function

\varphi:s\mapsto\textnormal{dim}_{k(s)}\ \mathcal{M}_{1,s}\cap\mathcal{M}_{2,s}

(166)

takes a constant value
r
on some dense open subset
U
of
X
. We will say that the intersection
\mathcal{M}_{1}\cap\mathcal{M}_{2}

generically has rank
r
.

Definition 6.1

(The slope of a vector bundle)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be any subbundle of rank
r
contained in
\omega_{i}
. We define
the slope of
\mathcal{F}
on
T
of order
s
as the sequence of integers

\vec{r}_{s}(T,\mathcal{F}):=(r_{1},r_{2},\dots,r_{sN}).

(167)

Here, the integers
r_{a}
are defined inductively as follows.

\bullet
We take
\mathcal{F}_{0}:=\mathcal{F}
,
\tilde{r}_{0}=r
, and
T_{0}:=T
.

\bullet
For
1\leq j\leq sN-1
, assume that
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\subseteq\tilde{\omega}_{i-j+1}
and
T_{j-1}\subseteq T
are defined, where
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}
is a subbundle of
\tilde{\omega}_{i-j+1}
of rank
\tilde{r}_{j-1}
on
T_{j-1}
. Here, the subscript for
\tilde{\omega}
is again viewed as modulo
N
. Then it is reasonable to talk about
V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{j-1}^{(p)})
, and we define

\tilde{r}_{j}:=\textnormal{The generic intersection rank of $\tilde{\omega}_{i-j}\cap V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{j-1}^{(p)})$ on $T_{j-1}$}.

We define

\displaystyle T_{j}=\textnormal{A dense open subset of $T_{j-1}$ over which the above intersection has generic rank}.

(168)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{j}:=\tilde{\omega}_{i-j}\cap V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{j-1}^{(p)})\textnormal{ on $T_{j}$},\textnormal{ which is a subbundle of $\tilde{\omega}_{i-j}$},

\displaystyle r_{j}:=\tilde{n}_{i-j+1}+\tilde{r}_{j-1}-\tilde{r}_{j}.

\bullet
For
j=sN
, we define

\tilde{r}_{sN}:=\textnormal{The generic intersection rank of }\mathcal{F}_{(s-1)N}\cap V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{(p)}_{sN-1})\textnormal{ on }T_{sN-1}.

(169)

We define

\displaystyle T_{sN}=\textnormal{A dense open subset of }T_{sN-1}\textnormal{ over which the above intersection has generic rank}.

(170)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{sN}:=\mathcal{F}_{(s-1)N}\cap V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{sN-1}^{(p)})\textnormal{ on }T_{sN},\textnormal{ which is a subbundle of }\mathcal{F}_{(s-1)N},

\displaystyle r_{sN}:=\tilde{n}_{i+1}+\tilde{r}_{sN-1}-\tilde{r}_{sN}.

If
\mathcal{E}\supseteq\omega_{i}
, we define
\vec{r}_{s}(T,\mathcal{E})
as
\vec{r}_{s}(T,\mathcal{E}^{\perp})
by working on the conjugate side.

Remark 6.2
.

The choice of the open subsets
T_{j}
’s does not affect our definition. Hence, we can always take
T_{j}
to be the largest locus over which the intersection attains its generic rank.

Definition 6.3

(Total slope)
.

Assumptions as above, we define
the total slope of
\mathcal{F}
on
T
of order
s
to be

r_{s,\textnormal{tot}}(T,\mathcal{F}):=\sum_{j=1}^{sN}r_{j}.

(171)

Definition 6.4

(Order of slopes)
.

For vector bundles
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{E}\subseteq\omega_{i}
, we define an order between
\vec{r}_{s}(T,\mathcal{F})
and
\vec{r}_{s}(T,\mathcal{E})
by
lexicographical order
. Note that the slopes are comparable only when the vector bundles are both subbundles of the same
\omega_{i}
. We define a total order on the set of total slopes using the natural order.

Remark 6.5
.

In this paper, we only need to use slopes of order 1 for the sufficiency part of the ampleness criterion. Hence, unless otherwise stated, we always assume
s=1
and drop the subscript
s
from our notions.

6.2 Illustration of slopes.

The definition of slopes may seem indirect. However, we introduce a method to appropriately represent the combinatorical data in a diagram, in which our notion of slopes is the slopes of the connecting lines between the vertices.

Step 1: Draw the nodes.
We consider the Cartesian coordinate system.

\bullet
Put
0
at
(1,0)
, and
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{j}
at
(1,j)
for all
1\leq j\leq n
. We finish the construction of the first column.

\bullet
Next, suppose that the first
u
rows have already been drawn, and
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{u}
is placed at
(u,v)
, then we place
\tilde{\omega}_{u+1}
at
(u+1,v)
.

\bullet
For all
l
, place
\mathcal{F}^{u+1}_{l}
at
(u+1,v-m_{u+1}+l)
. We then finish the
(u+1)
-th column.

\bullet
Conclude the inductive process at the
(N+1)
-th column.

\bullet
Erase the points that correspond to the vector bundles
\mathcal{F}^{u}_{l}
that contain
\tilde{\omega}_{u}
for all
1\leq u\leq N+1
.

\bullet
Draw segments connecting the top (resp. bottom) elements of adjacent columns.

We remark that in the definition of slopes, we work on any scheme
T
over
X
. We do not require
T
to record the refinement of the Hodge filtration, i.e.,
T
is not necessarily a scheme over the flag space
Y
. At this moment, one should think of the
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}
’s that we put in the diagram simply as some symbols, not genuine vector bundles on
T
.

Example 6.6
.

Let
G=G(U(2,1)\times U(3,0)\times U(1,2)\times U(0,3))
, the diagram becomes (Note that
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
differs from
\omega_{i}
only when
m_{i}=0
.)
\omega_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}
0
\omega_{2}
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{2}
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{1}
\omega_{3}
\mathcal{F}^{4}_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{4}_{2}
\mathcal{F}^{4}_{3}
\tilde{\omega}_{4}
\omega_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}
0

Step 2: Draw the lines.
To simplify the notations, we assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a subbundle of
\omega_{1}
of rank
l
and denote it by
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}
. By the definition of slope, there is a dense open subset
T_{N}
of
T
on which all intersections of vector bundles that we considered have generic ranks. We perform the following operations to the above diagram:

•

For
0\leq j\leq N
, select all the vertices
\mathcal{F}^{1-j}_{\tilde{r}_{j}}
in the diagram that correspond to the
\mathcal{F}_{j}
’s in our definition of slopes. Then we disregard all the other
\mathcal{F}^{u}_{v}
’s.

•

For
0\leq j\leq N-1
, draw line segments connecting
\mathcal{F}^{1-j}_{\tilde{r}_{j}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{-j}_{\tilde{r}_{j-1}}
. (The superscripts of
\mathcal{F}
are viewed as modulo
N
)

Our notion of the slope of
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}
corresponds to the sequence of slopes of line segments from the right to the left of the diagram.

Example 6.7
.

Let
X
be a unitary Shimura variety associated with
G=G(U(4,1)\times U(2,3)\times U(3,2))
. Consider the partial flag space
Y^{1}_{2}
over
X
, which records a subbundle
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
of rank 1 of
\omega_{1}
. The following diagram indicates that the slope of
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
on
Y^{1}_{2}
is
\vec{r}(Y^{1}_{2},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2})=(2,3,3)
.

\omega_{1}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{3}}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}}
0
\omega_{2}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{1}}
0
0
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{1}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{3}_{2}}
\omega_{3}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{3}}
\omega_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}}
0

In other words, the intersection
V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{2})\cap\omega_{3}
has generic rank 1 on
T_{N}
, and we denote this intersection by
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{1}
(One should pay attention that,
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{1}
only represents a vector bundle defined on
T_{N}
. This differs from the one in the definition of partial flag space
Y^{3}_{1}
, although we are using the same symbols), and the intersection
V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{3,(p)}_{1})\cap\omega_{2}
is trivial on
T_{N}
, and finally the intersection
V^{-1}(0)\cap\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
is trivial on
T_{N}
.

The following properties of slopes follow directly from the definition.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a subbundle of
\omega_{i}
over a scheme
T
, with
\vec{r}(T,\mathcal{F})=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N})
, then

(1)

r_{j}\geq 0
for all
j
.

(2)

r_{j}\leq\tilde{n}_{i-j}
for
1\leq j\leq N-1
.

(3)

r_{\textnormal{tot}}(T,\mathcal{F})\geq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{n}_{j}
, and the equality occurs if and only if the set of vector bundles
(\mathcal{F}_{j})_{j=1}^{N}
forms an
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain.

Proof:
Trivial.
\hfill\square

7  Proof of the ampleness criterion: The sufficiency part

7.1 Overview of the proof

The goal of this section is to prove the sufficiency part of our main theorems: Theorem
3.24
, Theorem
3.25
, Theorem
3.26
and Theorem
3.27
. The proof involves many inductive arguments as well as combinatorical choices. Before going into the proof, it is better to give an overview of the strategy of our proof in this subsection.

Convention 7.1
.

In our proof, we will frequently pull line bundles back via proper (surjective) morphisms
\pi:Z_{1}\to Z_{2}
. For simplicity, we will only write
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{L}|_{Z_{1}}
, or even just
\mathcal{L}
without specifying
\pi
for these pullbacks, as long as the meaning is obvious in the paragraph.

Convention 7.2
.

To simplify our notation, we drop the subscripts of
V_{es}
and
F_{es}
and simply write
V
and
F
. Similarly, we use
\omega_{i}
to denote the image of
essential
Verschiebung and
m_{i}
to be the rank of
\omega_{i}
. (Originally we used
\tilde{\omega}_{i}
and
\tilde{m}_{i}
for these terms)

In Section 7.2, we reduce the sufficiency part of the ampleness criterion to the corresponding nefness criterion of the
minimal partial flag spaces
. More explicitly, to prove the sufficiency part of Theorem
1.6
, it suffices to prove the sufficiency part of the following theorems.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
P^{\prime}=P^{i}_{j}
be a minimal proper parabolic subgroup, and
Y_{P^{\prime}}=Y^{i}_{j}
be the corresponding partial flag space obtained by recording a rank
j
vector bundle contained in (or containing, depending on
j
)
\omega_{i}
in the moduli problem. Let

\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)=\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\alpha)=\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq i\end{subarray}}^{N}(\textnormal{det }\omega_{l})^{\otimes k_{l}}\otimes\big(\textnormal{det }(\tilde{\omega}_{i}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})\big)^{\otimes k_{i}}\otimes(\textnormal{det }\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})^{\otimes\alpha}

be the automorphic line bundle of weight
\lambda
over
Y^{i}_{j}
.

1.

If
T
is the empty set, then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\alpha)
is nef if and only if

k_{i}\geq\alpha.

(172)

From now on we assume that
T
is non-empty, then there is a unique
1\leq t_{0}\leq t
, such that
i\in\{i_{t_{0}},i_{t_{0}}+1,\dots,i_{t_{0}+1}-1\}
. To simplify the notation, we assume that
i_{1}=i_{t_{0}}\leq i
.

2.

If
T
is a singleton, namely,
T=\{i_{1}\}
. If moreover
m_{i_{0}}=n-1
, then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\alpha)
is nef if and only if

\displaystyle k_{i}\geq\alpha,

(173)

\displaystyle(p^{(n-j)N}-1)\big(k_{i_{1}}-\frac{1}{p^{i-i_{1}}}(k_{i}-\alpha)\big)\geq(p^{(n-j-1)N}-1)k_{i_{1}}.

3.

If
T
is a singleton, namely,
T=\{i_{1}\}
. If moreover
m_{i_{0}}=1
, then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\allowbreak\alpha)
is nef if and only if

\displaystyle k_{i}\geq\alpha,

(174)

\displaystyle(p^{jN}-1)\big(k_{i_{1}}-\frac{1}{p^{i-i_{1}}}(k_{i}-\alpha)\big)\geq(p^{(j-1)N}-1)k_{i_{1}}.

4.

In the rest of the cases, we have
t\geq 2
, or
t=1
but
m_{i_{1}}\notin\{1,n-1\}
. Then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\allowbreak\alpha)
is nef if and only if

\displaystyle k_{i}\geq\alpha,

(175)

\displaystyle p^{a(i_{1})}\big(k_{i_{1}}-\frac{1}{p^{i-i_{1}}}(k_{i}-\alpha)\big)\geq k_{i_{1}+1},

\displaystyle p^{a(i_{s})}k_{i_{s}}\geq k_{i_{s+1}},\qquad\textnormal{for }2\leq s\leq t.

We will also prove the nefness criterion for certain line bundles over the Shimura variety
X
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Write
T=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{t}\}
. The line bundle

\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)=\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}\})=\bigotimes_{l=1}^{N}(\textnormal{det }\omega_{l})^{\otimes k_{l}}

over
X
is nef if and only if

p^{a(i_{s})}k_{i_{s}}\geq k_{i_{s+1}}

(176)

holds for
1\leq s\leq t
.

Notice that we simply change all the strict inequalities "
>
" to "
\geq
" for the nef cones. This agrees with the fact that the nef cone is the closure of the ample cone.

We prove Theorem
7.3
and Theorem
7.4
simultaneously. The strategy, roughly speaking, is by induction with respect to the essential degree
t=\#T
. The following diagram illustrates how our inductive process works:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=4$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\cdots\cdots\cdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=3$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=3$ and $i\in T$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=2$ and $i\notin T$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=2$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefleness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=2$ and $i\in T$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=1$ and $i\notin T$}\end{subarray}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefleness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=1$ and $i\in T$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)$}\\
\textnormal{when $t=0$ and $i\notin T$}\end{subarray}}

Here, each block means that "we prove the nefness of the line bundle whose weight satisfies the given the corresponding inequality in Theorem
7.3
and Theorem
7.4
, under the inductive hypothesis that the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion corresponding to the previous blocks holds". The weight
\lambda
has different meanings for each block.

The case
t=0
is the nefness criterion for line bundles over flag varieties (in characteristic
p
); The nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
when
t=1
is the ampleness of Hodge line bundle. We do not include these two trivial cases in the diagram. The reader should also pay attention to the essential degree
t
on the rightmost column.

In Sections 7.4, 7.5 and 7.6, we prove the nefness criterion corresponding to the left, middle and right columns respectively. This is the core of our proof.

Strategy of the proof:
The basic idea of the proof is to analyze the intersection number

\big(\mathcal{L}\cdot C\big)

(177)

for
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\lambda)
or
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
, where
\lambda
satisfies the numerical conditions for the nef cone, and
C
is any complete curve over
Y
or
X
. We try to write the class
[\mathcal{L}]
as a non-negative linear combination of certain divisors and nef classes.

[\mathcal{L}]=\sum[h_{i}]+[\mathcal{L}_{\textnormal{nef}}].

(178)

Here, each
h_{i}
is a "generalized strata partial Hasse invariant", whose zero locus is an Eckdahl–Oort strata of lower dimension.
\mathcal{L}_{\textnormal{nef}}
is (the pullback of) an automorphic line bundle of an appropriate choice of weight, which is known to be nef by induction hypothesis. Such an expression immediately implies

\big(\mathcal{L}\cdot C\big)\geq 0,

(179)

as long as
C
is not contained in the zero locus of any strata Hasse invariant
h_{i}
. If
C
is contained in some
Z(h_{i})
, then we repeat this process by a new choice of strata Hasse invariants and the nef line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\textnormal{nef}}
. This helps us to reduce the dimension and eventually deduce the non-negativity of the intersection numbers.

Remark 7.5
.

As is explained above, in order to establish a desired decomposition of (the class of) a given automorphic line bundle, one has to overcome two technical difficulties: Construction of partial Hasse invariants over strata and resolution of singularities of certain Ekedahl–Oort strata. Both problems are crucial to understand the geometry and cohomology of the special fiber of Shimura varieties. There are only partial results for both questions. We refer to the introduction part of this paper for a survey of some relevant research. In our proof, we make use the moduli interpretation to construct strata Hasse invariants, and also resolve certain singular closed Ekedahl–Oort strata by working on a partial flag space given by more refined data.

To prove the statements for the left column, it suffices to show that the weights on the boundary of the conjectured cone are nef weights. For example, we shall prove that
\lambda_{0}=(1,p^{a(i_{1})},p^{a(i_{1})+a(i_{2})},\dots,p^{a_{i_{1}}+\cdots+a(i_{t-1})})
is a nef weight. To attack this problem, we study the so-called
Schubert cells
(see Subsection 7.4 for the explicit definition), which are closures of certain Ekedahl–Oort strata. These closed subschemes are defined by explicit conditions in terms of the rank of intersections of
\omega_{i_{j}}
and
\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{j})}
. Through these moduli description, it is easy to construct strata Hasse invariants which cut out lower dimensional Schubert cells. This enables us to establish the desired equalities as above.

To prove the statement for the middle and right columns, we make induction on "slopes of
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}
". The rough picture for the proof is

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)|_{Z_{0}}$}\\
\textnormal{where $r_{\textnormal{tot}}(Z_{0},\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})=\sum_{j=1}^{N}n_{j}$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)|_{Z_{1}}$}\\
\textnormal{where $\vec{r}(Z_{1},\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})>\vec{r}(Z_{0},\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})$}\end{subarray}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{nefness of $\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)|_{Z}$}\\
\textnormal{where $\vec{r}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j})$ is generic}\end{subarray}}

In other words, we construct strata Hasse invariants, compute intersection numbers
(\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)\cdot C)
, and reduce the problem to lower dimensional subschemes over which the slope of
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}
becomes smaller. A crucial remark is that, by each block, we mean proving the nefness of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)
for all
j
’s simultaneously. For example, to prove
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\lambda)
is nef over
Z_{1}
, we have to use the inductive hypothesis that
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{\prime}}}(\lambda^{\prime})|_{Z_{0}}
is nef, provided that both
\lambda
and
\lambda^{\prime}
belong to their respective nef cones.

In subsection 7.3, we prove the induction base for all our future inductions.

Now, let us state the induction hypothesis for this whole chapter.

Definition 7.6
.

Let
X
be the geometric special fiber of a unitary Shimura variety, and let
Y^{i}_{j}
be a minimal partial flag space over
X
. Denote by
T
the essential set of
X
and
t=\#T
the essential degree of
X
. For any proper morphism
\pi:W\to Y^{i}_{j}
, we define the tuple

\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\big(t,\vec{r}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}),j\big),\qquad&&\textnormal{if }i\in T\\
&\big(t+1,\vec{r}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{j}),j\big),\qquad&&\textnormal{if }i\notin T.\end{aligned}\right.

(180)

Similarly, for any proper morphism
W\to X
, we define

\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)):=\big(t,(0,\dots,0),0\big).

(181)

Finally, let
\mathfrak{I}
be the set of all such tuples, and we define the order on
\mathfrak{I}
as the lexicographical order.

In the following subsections, we prove the ampleness criterion for
all
unitary Shimura varieties simultaneously by induction with respect to the partial order on
\mathfrak{I}
. More explicitly,

Induction hypothesis:
To prove that
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)|_{W}
(or
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\mu)
) is nef if the weight
\lambda
(or
\mu
) satisfies the corresponding inequalities in Theorem
7.3
and Theorem
7.4
, we assume that
\mathcal{L}_{{Y^{\prime}}^{i^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}}(\lambda^{\prime})|_{Z}
and
\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime\prime})|_{Z}
is nef as long as the weight
\lambda^{\prime}
and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}
satisfies the inequalities in the same theorems, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda))\textnormal{ or }\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{X}(\mu))>\mathfrak{I}(Z,\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\prime i^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}}(\lambda^{\prime})),

(182)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda))\textnormal{ or }\mathfrak{I}(W,\mathcal{L}_{X}(\mu))>\mathfrak{I}(Z,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime\prime})).

Here,
X^{\prime}
runs over the special fiber of unitary Shimura varieties of all possible signatures and
Y^{\prime i^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}
is a minimal partial flag space over
X^{\prime}
. Please note that we prove the sufficiency part of the ampleness criterion for all unitary Shimura varieties simultaneously.

Since the index set is rather complicated, we will write down the corresponding assumptions in each subsection accordingly.

7.2 Step 1: Reduction to the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion.

Lemma 7.7
.

If the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}\})
) holds, then the sufficiency part of the ampleness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}\})
) holds.

Proof:
The Hodge line bundle
\textnormal{det $\omega$}=\otimes_{i=1}^{N}\textnormal{det $\omega_{i}$}
is ample, which corresponds to the weight
\lambda_{0}=\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{N}=\allowbreak\{1,1\dots,1\}
. Given any
\{k_{l}\}
in the ample cone for
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
, since the ample cone is an open subset of
\mdmathbb{R}^{N}
, there exists an
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
such that

[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}\})]=[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}^{\prime}\})]+\varepsilon[\textnormal{det $\omega$}]

(183)

in
\textnormal{(Pic $X$)}_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
and
\{k_{l}^{\prime}\}
also lies in the ample cone. By Proposition
4.16
, it suffices to prove
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}^{\prime}\})
is nef. Similarly, we can take an arbitrary
L
-regular dominant weight
\lambda
, the automorphic line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is therefore relatively ample (i.e., fiberwise ample) with respect to the projection
Y\to X
. Thus, for
M\gg 1
, the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda_{0})\otimes\pi^{\ast}(\textnormal{det }\omega)^{\otimes M}
is ample
[
34
]
. Again since the conjectured ample cone for
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is an open cone, there exists
0<\epsilon\ll 1
such that

[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)]=[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda^{\prime})]+\epsilon[\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda_{0})\otimes\pi^{\ast}(\textnormal{det $\omega$})^{\otimes M}]

(184)

in
(\textnormal{Pic $Y$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
and
\lambda^{\prime}
lies in the conjectured ample cone. Tt suffices to prove
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda^{\prime})
is nef.
\hfill\square

Let
C_{\textnormal{nef}}
denote the conjectured nef cone, i.e., the closure of the cone in Theorem
1.6
. This is a convex cone in
\mdmathbb{R}^{nN}
. By Lemma
3.21
, we have
[\textnormal{det }H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}]=0
in
(\textnormal{Pic $Y$}){Q}
, so we could view the nef cone as in
\mdmathbb{R}^{N(n-1)}
by restricting to the intersection of hyperplanes

\sum_{j=1}^{n}k^{i}_{j}=0

(185)

for all
1\leq i\leq N
. A convex cone is always generated by its boundary rays. For each case in Theorem
1.6
, forcing
N(n-1)-1
inequalities to be equalities and leaving exactly one inequality unchanged gives rise to a boundary ray of this cone. It is easy to see that any weight
\lambda
lying in some boundary ray is a parallel weight for either
L
or some
L^{i}_{j}
. This argument implies

Lemma 7.8
.

In order to prove
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
is nef for
\lambda\in C_{\textnormal{nef}}
, it suffices to prove for
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)
of either form:

\bullet

\lambda
is a parallel weight for
L
