, and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{l}\})
;

\bullet

\lambda
is a parallel weight for
L^{i}_{j}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}(\lambda)=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\lambda)=\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{j}}(\{k_{l}\};\alpha)
.
\hfill\square

By Lemma
4.16
, we are reduced to proving the sufficency part of Theorem
7.3
and Theorem
7.4
.

7.3 Step 2: The induction base.

As described in subsection 7.1, the proof will heavily rely on inductions on the set
\mathfrak{I}
. In this part, we prove the base case for all induction steps:

Lemma 7.9
.

If the essential degree
t=0
, then the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{\lambda})
holds.

Proof:
This follows directly from the regular dominance of
\lambda
and the standard results of flag varieties in positive characteristics; see
[
5
]
, for example.
\hfill\square

Lemma 7.10
.

If
T=\{i_{1}\}
(that is,
t=1
) and
m_{i_{1}}=n-1
. For simplicity, we assume
i_{1}=1
. For any
1\leq l\leq n-1
Let
Z\subseteq Y^{1}_{l}
denote the subvariety defined by

[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}\subseteq V^{-N}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p^{N})}_{l})].

(186)

Then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)|_{Z}=\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(k_{1};\alpha)|_{Z}
is nef if

\displaystyle k_{1}\geq\alpha,

(187)

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle p^{N(n-l)}-1)\alpha\geq(p^{N(n-l-1)}-1)k_{1}.

Proof:
The goal is to prove that the intersection

\big(k_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]+\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]\cdot C\big)\geq 0

(188)

for any complete curve
C\subseteq Z
under the numercial condition above. We prove the statement of the proposition simultaneously for
1\leq l\leq n-1
.

Consider a sequence of nested strata within
Z
:

Z_{n-l-1}\subseteq Z_{n-l-2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Z_{1}\subseteq Z_{0}:=Z,

(189)

where
Z_{j}
for
j\geq 1
is defined by the condition

Z_{j}:=[\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1}\subseteq V^{-N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N})})\subseteq V^{-2N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{2N})})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq V^{-jN}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{jN})})\subseteq\omega_{1}]\subseteq Z_{j-1}.

(190)

Remark 7.11
.

In the above moduli interpretation, each
V^{-jN}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{jN})})
is not necessarily a vector bundle on
Z
, since
V^{-N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N})})
fails to be contained in
\omega_{1}=\textnormal{Im }V^{N}
, so it is not well-defined to consider the inverse image under the (essential) Verschiebung map. However, on each stratum
Z_{j-1}
, we have
V^{-(j-1)N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{(j-1)N})})\subseteq\omega_{1}
, and
V^{-jN}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{jN})})
is a well-defined vector bundle of rank
l+j
on
Z_{j-1}
. Therefore, the correct way to understand the condition is that the
Z_{j}
’s are consecutively defined by adding one new condition to
Z_{j-1}
.

Lemma 7.12
.

Each
Z_{j}
is a divisor in
Z_{j-1}
. The class of
Z_{j}
in
\textnormal{Pic}(Z_{j-1})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
is equal to

(p^{jN}-1)[\omega_{1}]-(p^{jN}-p^{(j-1)N})[\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1}].

Proof:

Z_{j}
is the zero locus of the section

h_{j}:V^{-jN}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{jN})})/V^{-(j-1)N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{(j-1)N})})\longrightarrow H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}/\omega_{1}.

(191)

Here, the map is induced by the natural inclusion. Again, by Lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle c_{1}(h_{j})

\displaystyle=c_{1}\big(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}/\omega_{1}\big)-c_{1}\big(V^{-jN}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{jN})})/V^{-{(j-1)N}}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p^{(j-1)N})}_{l})\big)

(192)

\displaystyle=-[\omega_{1}]-p^{(j-1)N}[V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N})})/\mathcal{F}_{l}]=(p^{jN}-1)[\omega_{1}]-(p^{jN}-p^{(j-1)N})[\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1}]

in Pic
(Z_{j-1}){Q}
.
\hfill\square

Now for any complete curve
C\subseteq Z
, let
j
be the largest non-negative integer such that
C\subseteq Z_{j}
. If
j<n-l-1
, this means
C\nsubseteq Z(h_{j+1})
, and

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}-\alpha}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}[h_{j+1}]+\big(k_{1}-\frac{p^{(j+1)N}-1}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)[\omega_{1}]

(193)

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}-\alpha}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}[h_{j+1}]+\frac{(p^{(j+1)N}-1)\alpha-(p^{jN}-1)k_{1}}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}[\omega_{1}].

By the assumption
(p^{(n-l)N}-1)k_{l}\geq(p^{(n-l-1)N}-1)k_{n-1}
, a direct computation implies that the coefficient of the second term is non-negative. Combining this with the nefness of
[\omega_{1}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{L}_{Y_{l}}(k_{n-1}-k_{l})\cdot C\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{k_{n-1}-k_{l}}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}[h_{j+1}]+\frac{(p^{(j+1)N}-1)k_{l}-(p^{jN}-1)k_{n-1}}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}[\omega_{1}]\cdot C\big)

(194)

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{n-1}-k_{l}}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}\big([h_{j+1}]\cdot C\big)+\frac{(p^{(j+1)N}-1)k_{l}-(p^{jN}-1)k_{n-1}}{p^{(j+1)N}-p^{jN}}\big([\omega_{1}]\cdot C\big)\geq 0.

If
j=n-l-1
, i.e.,
C\subseteq Z_{n-1-l}
. This stratum is defined by the condition

Z_{n-1-l}:=[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}\subseteq V^{-N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N})})\subseteq V^{-2N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{2N})})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq V^{-(n-1-l)N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{(n-l-1)N})})\subseteq\omega_{1}].

(195)

Note that every time we take
V^{-N}
, the rank increases by 1, hence the last inclusion is in fact an equality of the vector bundles. By Lemma
5.2
, we have the following in
(\textnormal{Pic $Z_{n-l-1}$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle=[V^{-(n-1-l)N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{(n-l-1)N})})]=p^{N}[V^{-(n-2-l)N}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{(n-l-2)N})})/\omega_{1}]

(196)

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=p^{(n-1-l)N}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]-\big(p^{N}+p^{2N}+\cdots+p^{(n-1-l)N}[\omega_{1}]\big).

We can express
[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]
in terms of
[\omega_{1}]
and plug into
[\mathcal{L}_{Y_{l}}(k_{1};\alpha)]
:

\displaystyle\big|_{Z_{n-l-1}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(k_{1}-\frac{p^{(n-l)N}-1}{p^{(n-1-l)N}(p^{N}-1)}(k_{1}-\alpha)\bigg)[\omega_{1}]

(197)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{(n-1-l)N}(p^{N}-1)}\Big((p^{(n-l)N}-1)\alpha-(p^{(n-1-l)N}-1)k_{1}\Big)[\omega_{1}].

The non-negativity of the coefficient is exactly our assumption.
\hfill\square

We remark that in the case where
T=\{i_{1}\}
and
m_{i_{1}}=1
, the proof of the corresponding statement is similar. Actually, by working on the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}^{c}
-side, the proof is exactly the same.

Lemma 7.13
.

If
T=\{i_{1}\}
(that is,
t=1
) and
m_{i_{1}}\neq\{1,n-1\}
. For simplicity, we assume
i_{1}=1
. For any
1\leq l\leq m_{1}
Let
Z\subseteq Y^{1}_{l}
denote the subvariety defined by

[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}\subseteq V^{-N}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p^{N})}_{l})].

(198)

Then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)|_{Z}=\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(k_{1};\alpha)|_{Z}
is nef if

\displaystyle k_{1}\geq\alpha,

(199)

\displaystyle p^{N}\alpha\geq k_{1}.

Proof:
By assumption, the set

\big\{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l},V^{-(N-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N-1})}),\dots,V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p)})\big\}

(200)

forms an
F,V
-chain. Hence, by Proposition
4.12

Z
, up to Frobenius twists, admits a proper morphism to an auxiliary Shimura variety
X^{\prime}
.
X^{\prime}
also has essential degree
t^{\prime}=1
, with
T^{\prime}=\{2\}
and
m_{2}^{\prime}=m_{1}
. We can compute the first Chern class of
\omega_{2}^{\prime}
on
Z
as follows:

\displaystyle p[\omega^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle=[\omega^{\prime(p)}_{2}]=[V(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}/X^{\prime})_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}})]=[V{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}}/pV^{-(N-1)}({\mathcal{F}}_{l}^{1,(p^{N-1})})^{(p)}]

(201)

\displaystyle=\big[\frac{V\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}}{VpH_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}\big]+\big[\frac{VpH_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}{pV^{-(N-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{l}^{1,(p^{N-1})})^{(p)}}\big]

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]-p^{N-1}[V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{1,(p)}_{l})]\textnormal{ (Because the Verschiebung map are all isomorphisms except one)}

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]+p^{N}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}].

The terms in the computation should be viewed as their pointwise liftings to
W(k)
-lattices in
\tilde{D}(\mathcal{A})[\frac{1}{p}]
. One could also deduce the class by a careful analysis of the (kernel and image of the) universal isogeny. Therefore, we could write

\displaystyle\big|_{Z}

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]+\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}]=\frac{p}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[\omega_{2}^{\prime}]+\big(k_{1}-\frac{p^{N}}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)[\omega_{1}]

(202)

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{N}}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[\omega_{2}^{\prime}]+\frac{p^{N}\alpha-k_{1}}{p^{N}-1}[\omega_{1}].

Our assumption ensures that the coefficients are nonnegative, and since both
[\omega_{1}]
and
[\omega_{2}^{\prime}]
are classes of Hodge bundles on different Shimura varieties, we conclude that the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(k_{1};\alpha)
to
Z
is nef. The case
l>m_{1}
follows from a similar argument.

7.4 Step 3: The sufficiency part of
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)

In this step, our aim is to prove the following class

[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)]=[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{s}\})]=\sum_{s=1}^{t}k_{s}[\omega_{i_{s}}]

(203)

is a nef class under the hypothesis
p^{a(i_{s})}k_{s}\geq k_{s+1}
for all
1\leq s\leq t
. Here
T=\{{i_{1}},{i_{2}},\dots,{i_{s}}\}
with
i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{t}
, and the subscripts of
k
are viewed as modulo
t
. Notice that if
t=1
, then we need to show
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)=k_{1}[\omega_{i_{1}}]
is nef under the condition
k_{1}\geq 0
. This follows directly from the ampleness of Hodge line bundle
[
35
]
.

From now on, we assume the essential degree
t\geq 2
. The induction hypothesis in subsection 7.1, in particular, implies the following assumptions.

Induction hypothesis:
Theorem
7.3
holds for any minimal partial flag space over any Shimura variety with essential set
T^{\prime}
and essential degree
t^{\prime}=\#T^{\prime}
such that either

•

We have
i\in T^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}\leq t-1
, or

•

We have
i\notin T^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}\leq t-2
.

For unitary Shimura varieties with different signatures, it is in general not possible to talk about the relations of their nef cones, since they have different Hodge parabolics
P
’s, and
\lambda
corresponds to characters of different groups. However, for a fixed essential set
T
, note that
\lambda
corresponds to a tuple of numbers
\{k_{s}\}
, we can viewed it as a vector in
\mdmathbb{R}^{t}
. Under this identification, it is natural to define the
common
nef cone, denoted by
C_{T}
, as the intersection of the nef cones of all unitary Shimura varieties with essential set
T
.

Let
C_{\textnormal{SV}}
be the convex cone in
\mdmathbb{R}^{t}
defined by the condition

p^{a(i_{s})}k_{s}\geq k_{s+1},\qquad\textnormal{$1\leq s\leq t$}.

(204)

By the necessity part of the ampleness criterion, we know
C_{T}\subseteq C_{\textnormal{SV}}
, and the goal of this section is to prove the opposite inclusion.

Lemma 7.14
.

C_{\textnormal{SV}}
is spanned by the weights

\displaystyle(1,p^{a(i_{1})},p^{a(i_{1})+a(i_{2})},\dots,p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{t-1})}),

(205)

\displaystyle(p^{a(i_{2})+\cdots+a(i_{t})},1,p^{a(i_{2})},\dots,p^{a(i_{2})+\cdots+a(i_{t-1})}),

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots

\displaystyle(p^{a(i_{t})},p^{a(i_{t})+a(i_{1})},\dots,p^{a(i_{t})+\cdots+a(i_{t-2})},1).

Proof:
This follows from a straightforward linear algebra.
\hfill\square

In order to prove
C_{SV}\subseteq C_{T}
, it suffices to show each boundary weight lies in
C_{T}
. Without loss of generality, we work with
(1,p^{a(i_{1})},p^{a(i_{1})+a(i_{2})},\dots,p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{t-1})})
in the sequel. Let
\tilde{C}_{T},\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}\in\mdmathbb{R}^{t}
be the inverse images of
C_{T},C_{\textnormal{SV}}
under the map

\displaystyle\xi:\mdmathbb{R}^{t}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mdmathbb{R}^{t}

(206)

\displaystyle(k_{1},\dots,k_{n})

\displaystyle\longmapsto(k_{1},k_{2}p^{a(i_{1})},\dots,k_{d}p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{t-1})}).

The goal is to show the weight
(1,\dots,1)\in\tilde{C}_{T}
. Note that the ampleness of the Hodge bundle implies

(1,p^{-a(i_{1})},\dots,p^{-(a({i_{1}})+\cdots+a(i_{t-1}))})\in\tilde{C}_{T}.

(207)

Thus,
\tilde{C}_{T}
is non-empty. The following lemma is the heart of our proof. It produces new nef weights from the known ones, hence enlarging
\tilde{C}_{T}
:

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
\kappa=(k_{1},\dots,k_{t})\in\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
. If
\kappa\in\tilde{C}_{T}
, then for
j=1,\dots,t-1
,
\kappa_{j}^{\prime}=(k_{1},\dots,\frac{k_{j}+k_{j+1}}{2},\frac{k_{j}+k_{j+1}}{2},\dots,k_{t})\in\tilde{C}_{T}
.

Proof:
Let
C
be a complete curve, we aim to prove

([\mathcal{L}_{X}(\xi(\kappa_{j}^{\prime}))]\cdot C)\geq 0.

(208)

Definition 7.16
.

We call a closed subscheme of
X
defined by conditions of the form

\big\{\textnormal{dim }(\omega_{i_{l}}\cap\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{l})}_{es})\geq r_{i_{l}},1\leq l\leq t\big\}

a Schubert cell of
X
.

Note that
\omega_{i_{l}}
has rank
m_{i_{l}}
and
\textnormal{Ker }V_{i_{l},es}^{a(i_{l})}
has rank
n_{i_{l+1}}
, so
r_{i_{l}}
must be an integer within the interval

[\textnormal{Max}\{0,m_{i_{l}}-m_{i_{l+1}}\},\textnormal{Min}\{m_{i_{l}},n_{i_{l+1}}\}].

(209)

Note that if
r_{i_{l}}
is equal to the lower bound, then the condition at
i_{l}
is actually trivial.

Remark 7.17
.

The Scubert cells also satisfy properties such as non-emptiness, dimension formula, closure relations, etc, and forms a good stratification by considering the interior of each Schubert cell. On the other hand, since the Ekedahl–Oort stratification captures the relative position of the Hodge and conjugate filtrations, as well as a comparison isomorphism between the graded pieces, it is natural that Ekedahl–Oort stratification is finer than this Schubert-type stratification. In
[
29
]
, the author studied when the closure of an Ekedahl–Oort stratum agrees with a Schubert cell. We will not delve deeply into this discussion in this paper. It is enough to only consider the Schubert-type stratification for our purpose in this step.

Let
Z
be a Schubert cell of
X
. There are two possibilities for the conditions for
Z
: Either there exists some
l
, such that
r_{i_{l}}=\textnormal{Min}\{m_{i_{l}},m_{i_{l+1}}\}
, or there does not exist such an
l
.

Lemma 7.18
.

If
Z\subset X
is a Schubert cell of the first case, then
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)|_{Z}
is nef for any
\lambda\in C_{\textnormal{SV}}
.

Proof
. In this first case, the condition implies that
\omega_{i_{l}}\subseteq\textnormal{Ker }V^{a({i_{l}})}
or
\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{l})}\subseteq\omega_{i_{l}}
, depending on which vector bundle has the larger rank. Without loss of generality, suppose that we have
\omega_{i_{l}}\supseteq\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{l})}
for some
l
. The sequence

H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{1},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l}-1},\omega_{i_{l}},V\omega_{i_{l}},\dots,V^{a(i_{l})-1}\omega_{i_{l}},H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l+1}},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{N}

(210)

forms an
F,V
-chain. By Theorem
4.6
, the Schubert cell
Z
is, up to appropriate Frobenius twists, isomorphic to the minimal partial flag space
\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}
over the auxiliary unitary Shimura variety
\tilde{X}
. (We are actually a bit of sloppy here: Each sheaf
V^{r}\omega_{i_{l}}
is a subbundle of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l+1}+r}^{(p^{r})}
, so the above chain is actually not an
F,V
-chain. Nevertheless, there are two possible ways to modify this issue: (1) One could take the chain

0,\dots,0,\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{l})},\dots,\textnormal{Ker }V,0,\dots,0.

(211)

Then each
\textnormal{Ker }V^{r}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l+1}-r}
and one checks that this forms an
F,V
-chain. (2) We could generalize the notion of
F,V
-chain and allow each
\mathcal{E}_{i}
to be a subbundle of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}^{(p^{u_{i}})}
for some
p^{u_{i}}
-twist. Since being nefness is an equivalent condition pullbacks under proper surjective morphisms, it is harmless even if we allow these Frobenius twists. The reason that we choose the above sequence here is that it is intuitively more natural)

We collect some numerical information of
\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}
provided by Theorem
4.6
: Let
0\subseteq\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}\subseteq\mathcal{J}\subseteq H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}/\tilde{X})_{i_{l+1}}
denote the refinement of the Hodge filtration at
\tau_{i_{l+1}}
. Recall that each term in the above
F,V
-chain corresponds to the image of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}/\tilde{X})_{j}
for some
j
, and
\omega_{i_{l+1}}
corresponds to the image of
\mathcal{J}
, under the universal
p
-isogeny
\phi:\tilde{A}\to A
in the moduli problem of the correspondence.

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
(\tilde{m}_{j},\tilde{n}_{j})_{j\in\Sigma_{\infty}}
be the signature of
\tilde{X}
. Let
\tilde{\omega}_{j}=\omega_{\tilde{A}^{\vee}/\tilde{X},\tilde{\tau}_{j}}
for all
j
.

1.

(Signature changes)
\tilde{m}_{j}=m_{j}
for
j\notin\{i_{l},i_{l+1}\}
,
\tilde{m}_{i_{l}}=n
, and
\tilde{m}_{i_{l+1}}=m_{i_{l+1}}-n_{i_{l}}
. In particular, the essential set
\tilde{T}=T\backslash\{i_{l}\}
or
\tilde{T}=T\backslash\{i_{l},i_{l+1}\}
, depending on whether
m_{i_{l+1}}=n_{i_{l}}
.

2.

(Classes in Picard group) In
(\textnormal{Pic }\tilde{X})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
, we have
[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l}}]=0
,
[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}]=\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l})}}[\omega_{i_{l}}]+[\omega_{i_{l+1}}]
, and
[\omega_{j}]=[\tilde{\omega}_{j}]
for
j\notin\{i_{l},i_{l+1}\}
. Moreover, in
(\textnormal{Pic }\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
, we have
[\mathcal{J}]=[\omega_{i_{l+1}}]
.

Proof.
This is a direct application of Lemma
5.2
. We only explain the computation for
[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}]
here. Recall that

\displaystyle\phi_{i_{l},\ast}(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}/X)_{i})=\omega_{i_{l}},

(212)

\displaystyle\phi_{i_{l+1},\ast}\textnormal{ is an isomorphism}.

We have

[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}]=[\phi_{i_{l+1},\ast}(\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}})]=\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l})}}[V^{a(i_{l})}\omega_{i}].

(213)

A direct application of Lemma
3.21
to the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\textnormal{Ker }V^{a(i_{l})}\longrightarrow\omega_{i}\longrightarrow V^{a(i_{l})}\omega_{i}\longrightarrow 0

(214)

yields the result. The rest of the computations are similar.
\hfill\square

Now we have

\displaystyle\big|_{Z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{j}[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{j}}]=p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}]+(k_{l+1}-p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l})[\mathcal{J}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq l,l+1\end{subarray}}^{t}k_{j}[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{j}}]

(215)

\displaystyle=p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}/\mathcal{J}]+k_{l+1}[\mathcal{J}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq l,l+1\end{subarray}}^{t}k_{j}[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{j}}].

By the above lemma, either

•

The auxiliary Shimura variety
\tilde{X}
has essential degree
t^{\prime}=t-1
, and
\tilde{Y}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}^{i_{l+1}}
is flagged at an place in the essential set, or

•

The auxiliary Shimura variety
\tilde{X}
has essential degree
t^{\prime}=t-2
, and
\tilde{Y}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}^{i_{l+1}}
is flagged at an place not in the essential set.

Both satisfies the Induction hypothesis at the beginning of this subsection. By induction, it suffices to check
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)|_{Z}
satisfies the nef condition for
\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l+1}}}
. The inequalities away from
i_{l},i_{l+1}
are the same as the original ones. The dominance condition
-k_{l+1}\geq-p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}
for
\mathcal{J}
follows from the assumption. At
i_{l}
and
i_{l+1}
, the rest of the inequalities follow from a direct discussion:

(1) If
t\geq 3
and
m_{i_{l+1}}\neq n_{i_{l}}
. Then
t^{\prime}=t-1\geq 2
and

p^{a(i_{l-1})+a(i_{l})}k_{l-1}\geq p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l},\qquad p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}\geq k_{l+1},\qquad p^{a(i_{l+1})}k_{l+1}\geq k_{l+2}.

(216)

(2 )If
t=2
and
m_{i_{l+1}}\neq n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}=\{i_{l+1}\}
has cardinality 1 and
\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l}}}
is flagged at the place
\tilde{\tau}_{i_{l+1}}
. If moreover
\tilde{m}_{i_{l+1}}\in\{1,n-1\}
, then one checks

p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}\geq k_{l+1},\qquad((p^{N})^{r}-1)k_{l+1}\geq((p^{N})^{r-1}-1)p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}

(217)

holds for any
r\geq 1
. The latter inequality follows from (note that
a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})=N
in this case)

\frac{p^{Nr}-1}{(p^{N(r-1)}-1)p^{a(i_{l})}}\geq p^{a(i_{l+1})}.

(218)

(3) If
t=2
and
m_{i_{l+1}}\neq n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}=\{i_{l+1}\}
has cardinality 1 and
\tilde{Y}^{i_{l+1}}_{m_{i_{l}}}
is flagged at the place
\tilde{\tau}_{i_{l+1}}
. If moreover
\tilde{m}_{i_{l}}\notin\{1,n-1\}
, then one checks (note that
N=a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})
)

p^{N}k_{l+1}=p^{a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})}k_{l+1}\geq p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}.

(219)

(4) If
t\geq 4
and
m_{i_{l+1}}=n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}
has cardinality
\geq 2
. We have
[\tilde{\omega}_{i_{l+1}}]=[H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}/X)_{\tilde{\tau}_{i_{l+1}}}]=0
. The inequality

p^{a(i_{l-1})+a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})}\big(k_{l-1}+\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l-1})+a(i_{l})}}(k_{l+1}-p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l})\big)\geq k_{l+2}

(220)

holds because

p^{a(i_{l-1})}k_{l-1}\geq k_{l}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad p^{a(i_{l+1})}k_{l+1}\geq k_{l+2}.

(221)

(5) If
t=3
and
m_{i_{l+1}}=n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}=\{i_{l+2}\}
has cardinality 1. Assume further that
\tilde{m}_{i_{l+2}}\notin\{1,n-1\}
, then the inequality

p^{N}\big(k_{l+2}+\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l})+a(i_{l-1})}}(k_{l+1}-p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l})\big)\geq k_{l+2}

(222)

holds because
N=a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})+a(i_{l+2})
and the same reason as (4).

(6) If
t=3
and
m_{i_{l+1}}=n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}=\{i_{l+2}\}
has cardinality 1. Assume further that
\tilde{m}_{i_{l+2}}\notin\{1,n-1\}
, then it suffices to check

((p^{N})^{r}-1)\big(k_{l+2}+\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l+2})+a(i_{l})}}(k_{l+1}-p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l})\big)\geq((p^{N})^{r-1}-1)k_{l+2}

(223)

for any
1\leq r\leq n-1
. This is equivalent to (note that
N=a(i_{l})+a(i_{l+1})+a(i_{l+2})
in this case)

p^{a(i_{l+2})+a(i_{l})}(p^{Nr}-p^{N(r-1)})k_{l+2}+(p^{Nr}-1)k_{l+1}\geq(p^{Nr}-1)p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}.

(224)

The left hand side

\displaystyle p^{a(i_{l+2})+a(i_{l})}(p^{Nr}-p^{N(r-1)})k_{l+2}+(p^{Nr}-1)k_{l+1}\geq p^{a(i_{l})}(p^{Nr}-p^{N(r-1)})k_{l}+p^{-a(i_{l+1})-a(i_{l+2})}(p^{Nr}-1)k_{l}.

(225)

One checks that it is larger than the right hand side by comparing the coefficients.

(7) If
t=2
and
m_{i_{l+1}}=n_{i_{l}}
, then
\tilde{T}=\emptyset
and
\tilde{X}
is set of discrete points. The nefness corresponds to

p^{a(i_{l})}k_{l}\geq k_{l+1}.

(226)

Conclusively, we have shown that for any Schubert cell
Z
of the first case, the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
to
Z
is nef for any
\lambda\in C_{\textnormal{SV}}
. In particular,
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\xi(\kappa_{j}^{\prime}))|_{Z}
is nef. This completes the proof of Lemma
7.18
.
\hfill\square

Next, assume
Z
is a Schubert cell given by the conditions

\textnormal{dim }(\omega_{i_{l}}\cap\textnormal{Ker }V_{es}^{a(i_{l})})\geq r_{i_{l}},\qquad 1\leq l\leq t.

(227)

We moreover assume that it is of the second case. We first construct an auxliary scheme.

Construction:
Let
i_{l}\in T
,

\bullet
Take
\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}
to be a subbundle of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{i_{l}}
of rank
r_{i_{l}}
contained in both
\omega_{i_{l}}
and
\textnormal{Ker }V_{es}^{a(i_{l})}
. If
r_{i_{l}}=0
, then we make the trivial choice
\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}=0
;

\bullet
Take
\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}
to be a subbundle of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{i_{l}}
of rank
m_{i_{l}}+n_{i_{l+1}}-r_{i_{l}}
that contains both
\omega_{i_{l}}
and
\textnormal{Ker }V_{es}^{a(i_{l})}
. If
r_{i_{l}}=m_{i_{l}}-m_{i_{l+1}}
, then we make the trivial choice
\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}=H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{i_{l}}
.

If fact, we should write these auxiliary vector bundles as
\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}_{r_{i_{l}}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}_{m_{i_{l}}+n_{i_{l+1}}-r_{i_{l}}}
by our standard convention. However, since we only pick out one extra subbundle for each essential place, we prefer to record them in a simpler form.

For each
1\leq l\leq t
, the datum
(A,\lambda,\eta,\iota,\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}\subseteq\omega_{i_{l}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}})
corresponds to a stratum
Z_{i_{l}}
in the partial flag space
P^{i_{l},i_{l}}_{r_{i_{l}},m_{i_{l}}+n_{i_{l+1}}-r_{i_{l}}}
. Furthermore, the forgetful map
Z_{i_{l}}\to Z
is projective and surjective. We are reduced to prove that the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
to
Z_{i_{l}}
is nef.

The sequences

\displaystyle 0,\dots,0,\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}},V_{es}\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}},\dots,V_{es}^{a(i_{l})-1}\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}},0,\dots,0,

(228)

\displaystyle H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{1},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l}-1},\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}},V\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}},\dots,V^{a(i_{l})-1}\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}},H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i_{l+1}},\dots,H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{N}

are both
F,V
-chains (Here again, we need to allow Frobenius twists in the definition of
F,V
-chains). By Proposition
4.6
, we obtain correspondences of strata in partial flag spaces of unitary Shimura varieties for both
F,V
-chains.
W^{\prime}
Z_{i_{l}}
Z^{\prime}
X
X^{\prime}.
pr_{1}
pr_{2}
W^{\prime\prime}
Z_{i_{l}}
Z^{\prime\prime}
X
X^{\prime\prime}.
pr_{1}
pr_{2}

We denote the auxiliary unitary Shimura varieties by
X^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}
, and the universal abelian schemes by
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}/X^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}/X^{\prime\prime}
, respectively. We recall the signature conditions for
X^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}
as follows.

Lemma 7.20
.

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}/X^{\prime}
has signature condition
m_{j}^{\prime}=m_{j}
for
j\notin\{i_{l},i_{l+1}\}
,
m_{i_{l}}^{\prime}=m_{i_{l}}-r_{i_{l}}
and
m^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}=m_{i_{l+1}}+r_{i_{l}}
; Similarly,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}/X^{\prime\prime}
has signature condition
m_{j}^{\prime\prime}=m_{j}
for
j\notin\{i_{l},i_{l+1}\}
,
m_{i_{l}}^{\prime\prime}=m_{i_{l}+1}+r_{i_{l}}
and
m_{i_{l+1}}^{\prime\prime}=m_{i_{l}}-r_{i_{l}}
. In particular, both
X^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}
have essential set
T
.

Proof:
This follows directly from the construction via Dieudonné modules in Theorem
4.6
.
\hfill\square

Lemma 7.21
.

In
(\textnormal{Pic $X^{\prime}$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
, we have

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime}_{j}]=[\omega_{j}]=0,\quad\textnormal{if }j\notin T,

(229)

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime}_{j}]=[\omega_{j}],\quad\quad\ \ \ \textnormal{if }j\in T\textnormal{ but }j\neq i_{l},i_{l+1},

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime}_{i_{l}}]=[\omega_{i}]-[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}],

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime}_{i_{l+1}}]=\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l})}}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}]+[\omega_{i_{l+1}}].

The statement for
[\omega^{\prime\prime}_{j}]
in
(\textnormal{Pic $X^{\prime\prime}$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
is similar, with
\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}
replaced by
\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}
in each equation.

Proof:
These lemmas follow from a direct computation similar to Lemma
7.19
.
\hfill\square

For each
1\leq l\leq t
, we can construct a strata Hasse invariant on
Z
:

h_{i_{l}}:\textnormal{det }(\omega_{i_{l}}/\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}})\longrightarrow\textnormal{det }(\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}/\textnormal{Ker }V_{es}^{a(i_{l})}).

The zero locus of
h_{i_{l}}
is a lower-dimensional Schubert cell by the moduli description. In
(\textnormal{Pic $Z$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
, we have

[h_{i_{l}}]=p^{a(i_{l})}[\omega_{i_{l+1}}]-[\omega_{i_{l}}]+[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}]+[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}],

(230)

which follows from a direct application of Lemma
5.2
.

Let
C
be a complete curve in
X
. Let
Z
be the Schubert stratum of lowest dimension containing
C
. We can perform the above constructions for this particular
Z
. Note that the auxiliary unitary Shimura variety
X^{\prime}
has the same essential set
T
as
X
, we deduce that for any
\kappa\in\tilde{C}_{T}
and
1\leq l\leq t-1
, the class

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in T}k_{j}p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{j-1})}[\omega^{\prime}_{i_{j}}]

(231)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in T\backslash\{l,l+1\}}k_{j}p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{j-1})}[\omega_{i_{j}}]+k_{l}p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{l-1})}([\omega_{i_{l}}]-[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}])

\displaystyle+k_{l+1}p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{l})}\big(\frac{1}{p^{a(i_{l})}}[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}]+[\omega_{i_{l+1}}]\big)

is nef. Similarly for
X^{\prime\prime}
with
[\mathcal{F}^{i_{j}}]
replaced by
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{j}}]
. As before, we have the Hasse invariant

[h_{i_{l}}]=p^{a(i_{l})}[\omega_{i_{l+1}}]-[\omega_{i_{l}}]+[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}]+[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}].

Recall that in the statement of Lemma
7.15
, we expect to prove
\kappa_{l}^{\prime}\in\tilde{C}_{T}
. We can eliminate the classes
[\mathcal{F}^{i_{l}}]
and
[\mathcal{E}^{i_{l}}]
by

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}[\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\xi(\kappa))]+\frac{1}{2}[\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime\prime}}(\xi(\kappa))]+\frac{1}{2}(k_{l}-k_{l+1})p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{l-1})}[h_{i_{l}}].

(232)

By the choice of
Z
, we know that the curve
C\nsubseteq Z(h_{i_{l}})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\big([\mathcal{L}_{X}(\xi(\kappa_{j}^{\prime}))]\cdot C\big)=\frac{1}{2}\big([\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\xi(\kappa))]\cdot C\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big([\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime\prime}}(\xi(\kappa))]\cdot C\big)+\frac{1}{2}(k_{j}-k_{j+1})p^{a(i_{1})+\cdots+a(i_{j-1})}\big([h_{i_{j}}]\cdot C\big)\geq 0.

(233)

Here, from the definition of
\xi
, we see that the cone
\tilde{C}_{T}
is given by the condition

k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq\cdots\geq k_{t},\qquad p^{N}k_{t}\geq k_{1}.

(234)

This explains the non-negativity of the intersection number.

Combining the two cases together, we have shown that for any complete curve
C\subseteq X
,

\big(\mathcal{L}_{X}(\xi(\kappa_{l}))\cdot C\big)\geq 0,

(235)

i.e.,
\kappa_{l}\in\tilde{C}_{T}
, for any
1\leq l\leq t-1
. This completes the proof of Lemma
7.15
.
\hfill\square

Recall that we have the ampleness of the Hodge bundle, this is equivalent to

(1,p^{-a(i_{1})},\dots,p^{-(a(i_{1})+a(i_{2})+\cdots+a(i_{l-1}))})\in\tilde{C}_{T}.

(236)

Let
\tilde{C}_{0}
be the ray corresponding to the above class. We utilize the above lemma to improve our estimation of the cone of nef weights.

Proof of
\tilde{C}_{T}=\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
:
It suffices to prove that the left hand side contains the right hand side. Starting from the ray
\tilde{C}_{0}
, we apply Lemma
7.15
to all the tuples in
\tilde{C}_{0}
, and take the convex hull of
\tilde{C}_{0}
and the resulting set. We denote this new cone by
\tilde{C}_{1}
. By construction
\tilde{C}_{1}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{T}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
. We then repeat this procedure for
\tilde{C}_{1}
to obtain
\tilde{C}_{2}
, and continue this process iteratively. Consequently, we get a sequence of increasing convex subcones of
\tilde{C}_{T}
:

\tilde{C}_{0}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{1}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\tilde{C}_{T}.

(237)

The
k_{1}=1
slicing of the
\tilde{C}_{i}
forms an increasing sequence of closed subsets of the compact set
\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}\cap\{k_{1}=1\}
, hence the limit exists. Let
\tilde{C}
denote the convex cone of the closure of this limit. Since the nef condition is a closed condition, it follows that
\tilde{C}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{T}\subseteq\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
. Moreover,
\tilde{C}
has the property that for any tuple
(k_{1},\dots,k_{t})\in\tilde{C}
, the new tuple given by taking average of adjacent entries also lies in
\tilde{C}
.

Claim:
(1,\dots,1)\in\tilde{C}
.

Proof:
Let
\tilde{D}
denote the
k_{1}=1
slicing of
\tilde{C}
. The continuous function

f(1,k_{2},\dots,k_{t})=1-k_{t}.

(238)

attains its minimal value
f_{\textnormal{min}}
on the compact set
\tilde{D}
. Let

\tilde{E}=\{(1,\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{t})\in\tilde{D}\big|\ f(1,\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{t})=f_{\textnormal{min}}>0\}.

This is another compact set. Assume
(1,\dots,1)\notin\tilde{D}
. We define another function only on
\tilde{E}

g(1,\alpha_{2},\dots,\alpha_{t})=j,\quad\textnormal{if $\alpha_{2}=\cdots=\alpha_{j-1}=1$ but $\alpha_{j}<1$}.

(239)

This bounded function attains its minimal value
s
at some point
(1,\dots,1,\beta_{s},\dots,\beta_{t})
in
\tilde{E}
. However,

•

If
2<s<t
, then by Lemma
7.15
,

(1,\dots,\frac{1+\beta_{s}}{2},\frac{1+\beta_{s}}{2},\beta_{s+1},\dots,\beta_{t})\in\tilde{D}.

(240)

This tuple also lies in
\tilde{E}
, but
g
has values
s-1
on it, which contradicts the assumption on the minimality of
s
.

•

If
s=t
, again by Lemma
7.15
,

(1,\dots,\frac{1+\beta_{t}}{2})\in\tilde{D}.

(241)

However,
f
takes the value
\frac{1-k_{t}}{2}<1-k_{t}
on this tuple, contradicting the minimality of
\tilde{E}
.

•

If
s=2
, again by Lemma
7.15
,

(\frac{1+\beta_{2}}{2},\frac{1+\beta_{2}}{2},\dots,\beta_{t})\in\tilde{C}.

(242)

Since
\tilde{C}
is a cone, dividing by
\frac{1+\beta_{2}}{2}
yields

(1,1,\dots,\frac{2\beta_{n}}{1+\beta_{2}})\in\tilde{D}.

(243)

However,
f
takes the value
1-\frac{\beta_{t}}{1+\beta_{2}}<1-\beta_{t}
, contradicting the minimality of
\tilde{E}
.

We conclude that
(1,\dots,1)\in\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{nef}}
. In other words, we have shown that the weight

(1,p^{a(i_{1})},\cdots,p^{a(i_{1})+a(i_{2})+\cdots+a(i_{N-1})})

(244)

is nef. Since we could present the argument in this subsection to all boundary weights of
\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
, we have shown that
\tilde{C}_{T}=\tilde{C}_{\textnormal{SV}}
, and hence
C_{T}=C_{\textnormal{SV}}
. Consequently, this establishes the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda)
.
\hfill\square

7.5 Step 4: The sufficiency part of the nefness of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\lambda)
with
i\in T
.

For simplicity, we assume that
i=i_{1}=1\in T
and
m<m_{1}
. The proof is again based on analyzing the intersection number

\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\lambda)]\cdot C\big)

(245)

for any complete curve
C\subseteq Y^{1}_{m}
, or its pullback along any proper surjective morphism. The induction hypothesis in subsection 7.1, in particular, implies the following assumption.

Induction Hypothesis:

\bullet
The sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)
, where
1\leq l<m
, holds for any proper variety over
Y^{1}_{l}
where
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}
has slope
\leq\vec{r}_{0}
. More precisely, if
\pi:W\to Y^{1}_{l}
is a proper morphism such that
\vec{r}(Z,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l})\leq\vec{r}_{0}
, then
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)
is nef provided that
\lambda
satisfies the condition given by Theorem
7.3
.

\bullet
The sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)
, where
m\leq l\leq m_{1}
, holds for any proper variety over
Y^{1}_{l}
where
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l}
has slope
<\vec{r}_{0}
. More precisely, if
\pi:W\to Y^{1}_{l}
is a proper morphism such that
\vec{r}(Z,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{1}_{l})<\vec{r}_{0}
, then
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{l}}(\lambda)
is nef provided that
\lambda
satisfies the condition given by Theorem
7.3
.

Under these hypotheses, we prove that for any projective morphism
Z\to Y^{1}_{j}
, where
\vec{r}(Z,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{1}_{j})=\vec{r}_{0}\allowbreak=(r_{1},r_{2},\dots,r_{N})
, the pullback
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{j}}(\lambda)
to
Z
is nef if the weight
\lambda
satisfies the condition given by Theorem
7.3
.

For the proof, we first notice that there are two possibilities of
r_{\textnormal{tot}}
, according to Lemma
6.8
:

1.

Either
r_{\textnormal{tot}}>n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{N}
, or

2.

r_{\textnormal{tot}}=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
.

We first consider the case
r_{\textnormal{tot}}>n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
.

Construction 7.22
.

We construct a variety
\tilde{Z}
through a sequence of blow-ups via the following steps:

Step 1. Blow-ups in the direction of
V^{-1}
.

A.

Let
m^{1}_{N+1}(=m^{1}_{1})=m
. For
2\leq j\leq N
, assuming that
m^{1}_{j+1}
is defined, we set
m_{j}^{1}:=\allowbreak n-m_{j+1}+m_{j+1}^{1}-r_{N+1-j}
, where the subscripts are taken modulo
N
. Finally we take
m^{0}_{1}:=n-m_{2}+m^{1}_{2}-r_{N}
.

B.

Let
Z_{1}
be the closed subscheme of

Z\times_{X}Y^{N}_{m^{1}_{N}}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y^{2}_{m^{1}_{2}}\times_{X}Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}

(246)

defined by the condition

\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{1}_{j}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{1}_{j+1}}),\textnormal{ }\forall 1\leq j\leq N,\quad\textnormal{and }\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{m^{1}_{2}}).

(247)

(As we have already explained in the beginning of this section, we omit the symbol
\pi^{\ast}
from all our notations. The condition is in fact imposed on the pullback of these vector bundles to the fiber product scheme.)

Note that if
\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m^{1}_{j+1}}
is chosen, then
\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{1}_{j}}
will be uniquely determined on a dense open subset, as follows from the definition of slopes. The fiber over the complementary closed subset is isomorphic to a Grassmannian
P^{\prime}/P^{\prime\prime}
for some appropriate parabolic subgroups
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
of
G
. Hence, the above construction can be realized as a consecutive sequence of blow-ups.

Step 2. Blow-ups in the direction of
V
.

A.

For
l\geq 2
, suppose that for all
1\leq s\leq l-1
and
1\leq t\leq N
, the integers
m^{s}_{t}
are defined and the scheme
Z_{l-1}
has been constructed, whose moduli problem is given by recording subbundles
\mathcal{F}^{t}_{m^{s}_{t}}\subseteq\omega_{t}
or rank
m^{s}_{t}
, together with certain inclusion relations relevant to the essential Verschiebung map. Consider the slope of
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}
on
Z_{l-1}
. By construction, we have
\vec{r}(Z_{l-1},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}})\leq\vec{r}_{0}
.

•

If
\vec{r}(Z_{l-1},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}})<\vec{r}_{0}
, we terminate the inductive construction and set
\tilde{Z}=Z_{l-1}
;

•

Otherwise, we first set
m^{l}_{2}:=m^{l-1}_{1}+r_{N}+m_{2}-n
, and then set inductively
m^{l}_{j+1}:=\allowbreak m^{l}_{j}+r_{N+1-j}+m_{j+1}-n
for
2\leq j\leq N
.

B.

Let
Z_{l}
be the closed subscheme of

Z_{l-1}\times_{X}Y^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y^{N}_{m^{l}_{N}}

(248)

defined by the condition

\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}}),\ \forall 2\leq j\leq N,\quad\textnormal{and }\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{m^{l}_{2}}).

(249)

Lemma 7.23
.

We have

m^{l}_{1}-m^{l-1}_{1}=r_{\textnormal{tot}}-(n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{N}).

(250)

Proof.
This follows from our construction that

\displaystyle m^{l}_{1}-m^{l-1}_{1}

\displaystyle=(m^{l}_{1}-m^{l}_{N})+(m^{l}_{N}-m^{l}_{N-1})+\cdots+(m^{l}_{3}-m^{l}_{2})+(m^{l}_{2}-m^{l}_{1})

(251)

\displaystyle=(r_{1}-n_{1})+(r_{2}-n_{N})+\cdots+(r_{N-1}-n_{3})+(r_{N}-n_{2})

\displaystyle=r_{\textnormal{tot}}-(n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}).

\hfill\square

By the above lemma, one sees that step 2 must terminate at some
l=l_{0}
, since otherwise we would have

\vec{r}(Z_{l-1},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}})=\vec{r}_{0},

(252)

which forces
m^{l}_{1}>m^{l-1}_{1}
. That is, at the embedding
\tilde{\tau}_{1}
, we have subbundles
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}\subseteq\omega_{1}
of arbitrarily large rank, which is impossible.

When the inductive construction stops, we denote the resulting variety by
\tilde{Z}
. This completes our construction of the auxiliary variety
\tilde{Z}
.

Lemma 7.24
.

The forgetful map
\tilde{Z}\to Z
is proper surjective.

Proof:
It suffices to show that each morphism
Z_{l}\to Z_{l-1}
is surjective, which reduces to checking the induced map on closed points is surjective. For
2\leq j\leq N
, assume we have chosen
\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}
, then by the moduli problem, we need to choose
\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m_{j+1}^{l}}\subseteq\omega_{j}
such that

V\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}+\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l-1}_{j+1}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}}\subseteq\omega_{j}^{(p)}.

(253)

The choice of
\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m_{j+1}^{l}}
is generically unique, and the fiber over the complementary closed subscheme are Grassmannians
P^{\prime(p)}/P^{\prime\prime(p)}
for some parabolic subgroups. Thus,
Z_{l}
is obtained from
Z_{l-1}
by sequence of consecutive blow-ups and Frobenius twists.
\hfill\square

The basic idea of Construction
7.22
is to "draw parallelograms in the correct direction". We illustrate this construction with the following example.

Example 7.25
.

G=G(U(5,2)\times U(6,1)\times U(4,3))
. Let
Z/Y^{1}_{2}
be a projective variety where
\vec{r}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2})=(3,0,4)
. Then
\tilde{Z}
is obtained by further recording the following vector bundles and their inclusion relations, as indicated by the colored lines.

\omega_{1}
{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{4}}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{3}
{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}
0
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{1}}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{2}}
\cancel{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{3}}
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{4}
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{5}
\omega_{2}
0
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{2}
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{3}
\omega_{3}
0
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{1}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{3}
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{4}
\omega_{1}
\textcircled{1}
\textcircled{2}
\textcircled{3}
\textcircled{4}
\textcircled{5}
\textcircled{6}
\textcircled{7}
\textcircled{8}
\textcircled{9}

\bullet
The numbers on the arrows represent the order of construction. The red lines correspond to the first step, in which we start from
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{2}
on the right and draw lines "from right to left"; The blue and green lines correspond to the second step, where we take blow-ups up to Frobenius twists "from left to right".

\bullet
We have
V^{-1}(\omega_{2}^{(p)})\cap\mathcal{F}_{4}^{1}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{4}
, and the intersection is a vector bundle of rank
4
, indicated as the dotted arrow in the picture. Thus,
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{4})=(3,0,3)<\vec{r}
, and we end our construction after we record this vector bundle in our moduli problem.

\bullet
An alternative way to understand the terminal of our construction is that "we cannot draw a line segment starting at
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{4}
which is parallel to ⑦".

Next, we associate a "strata Hasse invariant" with each "parallelogram" in the picture of
\tilde{Z}
. Assume that
\tilde{Z}=Z_{l_{0}}
in our construction and let
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l_{0}}_{1}}
be the vector bundle of the highest rank at the first place that appears in the construction (which could be
\omega_{1}
, i.e.,
m^{l_{0}}_{1}=m_{1}
). For
2\leq l\leq l_{0}-1
we have the rank condition by construction:

\displaystyle\textnormal{rank $\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{N}^{l+1}}/\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m_{N}^{l}}$}

\displaystyle=(m^{l+1}_{N-1}+r_{2}+m_{N}-n)-(m^{l}_{N-1}+r_{2}+m_{N}-n)=m^{l+1}_{N-1}-m^{l}_{N-1}

(254)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{rank $\mathcal{F}^{N-1}_{m_{N-1}^{l+1}}/\mathcal{F}^{N-1}_{m_{N-1}^{l}}$}=\cdots=\textnormal{rank $\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{2}^{l+1}}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m_{2}^{l}}$}=\textnormal{rank $\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}$}.

The equality for
l=1
can be checked in a similar way. Thus, we obtain a section on this variety by taking
h^{l}_{j}
as the determinant of the following morphisms (The first morphism is induced by inclusion, and the second isomorphism is induced by the essential Verschiebung map)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{l}_{j}:\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l+1}_{j}}/\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l+1}_{j+1}})/V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m^{l+1}_{j+1}}/\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m^{l}_{j+1}}\big)^{(p)},\quad\textnormal{if $2\leq j\leq N$},

(255)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{l}_{1}:\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{m^{l+1}_{2}})/V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{m^{l}_{2}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m^{l+1}_{2}}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m^{l}_{2}}\big)^{(p)},\quad\textnormal{if $j=1$}.

Therefore, in
(\textnormal{Pic $\tilde{Z}$}){Q}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{1}(h^{l}_{j})=pc_{1}\big(\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m^{l+1}_{j+1}}/\mathcal{F}^{j+1}_{m^{l}_{j+1}})\big)-c_{1}\big(\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l+1}_{j}}/\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}\big),\quad\textnormal{if $2\leq j\leq N$},

(256)

\displaystyle c_{1}(h^{l}_{1})=pc_{1}\big(\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m^{l+1}_{2}}/\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m^{l}_{2}}\big)-c_{1}\big(\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}\big),\quad\textnormal{if $j=1$}.

"The
l
-th" partial Hasse invariant is defined as

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=p^{N-1}[h^{l}_{N}]+p^{N-2}[h^{l}_{N-1}]+\cdots+p[h^{l}_{2}]+[h^{l}_{1}]

(257)

\displaystyle=p^{N}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l+1}_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}]-[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l-1}_{1}}]\in(\textnormal{Pic $\tilde{Z}$}){Q}.

Remark 7.26
.

If
r_{j}=0
for some
j\geq 2
, then rank
V_{es}^{-1}({\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}}})=n_{j+1}+m^{l}_{j+1}=m^{l}_{j}=
rank
\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}
, and the inclusion condition is actually an equality
\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}=V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}})
. In this case, the corresponding section
\tilde{h}^{l}_{j}
is an isomorphism. The case for
j=1
is similar. Therefore, either
\tilde{h}^{l}_{j}
is a section of a line bundle whose zero section has codimension 1, or it is a nonzero section of the trivial bundle. We refer to both of them as "strata partial Hasse invariants".

Lemma 7.27
.

For
0\leq l\leq l_{0}-1
, we have
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}})\leq\vec{r}_{0}
. Besides,
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l_{0}}_{1}})<\vec{r}_{0}
.

Proof.
This follows directly from the construction.
\hfill\square

Lemma 7.28
.

For any
1\leq l\leq l_{0}-1
and
1\leq j\leq N
, let
Z_{j}^{l}
be the zero locus of
h^{l}_{j}
(if it is non-empty). Then
\vec{r}(Z^{l}_{j},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}})<\vec{r}_{0}
.

Proof:
Assume
2\leq j\leq N
for simplicity, the argument for
j=1
is similar. By definition, we have

\textnormal{rank }\big(\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l+1}_{j}}/\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}\cap V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}})/\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}\big)\geq 1

(258)

when viewed as subbundles of
V_{es}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l+1}_{j+1}})
. Consequently,

\textnormal{dim }\big(\omega_{j}\cap V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{l}_{j+1}})\big)\geq\textnormal{dim }\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{l}_{j}}+1=m^{l}_{j}+1>n+m^{l}_{j+1}-m_{j+1}-r_{j}.

(259)

Therefore, the slope of
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l}_{1}}
on
Z^{l}_{j}
is at most
\vec{r}^{\prime}=(r_{N},\dots,r_{j}-1,\ast,\dots,\ast)<\vec{r}
.
\hfill\square

We are now ready to prove that the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\lambda)
to
Z
is nef, under the induction hypothesis stated at the beginning of this subsection. Note that it suffices to prove

\big(\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)\cdot C\big)\geq 0

(260)

for any complete curve
C\subseteq\tilde{Z}
.

Let
C
be a complete curve on
\tilde{Z}
. We notice the following:

•

If
C
is contained in the zero locus of
h^{s}_{j}
for some
1\leq j\leq N
, let
1\leq s\leq l_{0}-1
be the smallest superscript of such
h^{s}_{j}
’s. By the above lemma,
\vec{r}(Z(h^{s}_{j}),\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}})<\vec{r}_{0}
. It follows from the induction hypothesis that the pullback of
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(k_{i};\alpha)]
to
Z(h^{s}_{j})
is nef, as long as the coefficients satisfy Theorem
7.3
;

•

If
C
is not contained in the zero locus of any
h^{s}_{j}
, we set
s=l_{0}
. By construction,
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l_{0}}_{1}})<\vec{r}_{0}
. By induction,
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(k_{i};\alpha)
is nef as long as the coefficients satisfy Theorem
7.3
.

•

Since we always have
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}})\leq\vec{r}
, the pullback of
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(k_{i};\alpha)]
is also nef as long as the coefficients satisfy Theorem
7.3
.

If
s=1
, then
h^{1}_{j}
vanishes for some
1\leq j\leq N
, and
C
is contained in a stratum where the slope of
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}
is strictly smaller than
\vec{r}
. By the inductive hypothesis, we obtain the non-negativity of the intersection numbers.

If
s\geq 2
, consider the positive linear combination

\displaystyle=[h^{s-1}]+\frac{p^{N}+1}{p^{N}}[h^{s-2}]+\frac{p^{2N}+p^{N}+1}{p^{2N}}[h^{s-3}]+\cdots+\frac{p^{(s-2)N}+\cdots+1}{p^{(s-2)N}}[h^{1}]

(261)

\displaystyle=p^{N}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}]-\frac{p^{sN}-1}{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{1}_{1}}]+\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}].

We use
[h]
to kill the coefficients of
[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]=[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{1}_{1}}]
and get

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(k_{i};\alpha)]

(262)

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]+\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]+\sum_{l=2}^{N}k_{l}[\omega_{l}]

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[h]-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}]-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}]+\sum_{l=1}^{N}k_{l}[\omega_{l}]

\displaystyle=\beta[h]+\bigg(tk_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}]+\big(tk_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}]+tk_{2}[\omega_{2}]+\cdots+tk_{N}[\omega_{N}]\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg((1-t)k_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}]+\big((1-t)k_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}]+(1-t)k_{2}[\omega_{2}]+\cdots+(1-t)k_{N}[\omega_{N}]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\beta[h]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(\{tk_{i}\};\alpha^{s}_{1})]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{(1-t)k_{i}\};\alpha^{0}_{1})].

In the last two equations, we wish to express the original class
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}]
as a linear combination of Hasse invariants and pullbacks of the classes
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}]
and
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}]
. To simplify the notation, we use
\beta,\alpha^{s}_{1},\alpha^{0}_{1}
to represent their corresponding coefficients.

Proposition 7.29
.

There exists a suitable choice of real numbers
\beta\geq 0
and
t\in[0,1]
fitting into the above equation, such that the weights for the line bundles
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(\{tk_{i}\};\alpha^{s}_{1})]
and
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{(1-t)k_{i}\};\alpha^{0}_{1})]
satisfy their respective nefness conditions given by Theorem
7.3
.

Proof.
By construction,

\beta=\frac{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\geq 0.

(263)

It remains to prove the statement for
t
. The proof is based on a case-by-case analysis.

Case 1:
If
\#T\geq 2
, then
\alpha
satisfies

k_{1}\geq\alpha,\qquad p^{a(1)}\alpha\geq k_{2},

(264)

and we must choose
t
such that (the remaining nefness conditions can be verified easily)

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big(tk_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle\geq tk_{2},

(265)

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big((1-t)k_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle\geq(1-t)k_{2}.

We could take the sum of the inequalities and obtain

p^{a(1)}(k_{1}-(k_{1}-\alpha))=p^{a(1)}\alpha\geq k_{2}.

(266)

This is valid because of the nefness condition for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)
. Thus, there exists some
t
that satisfies both inequalities. We claim that such
t
must lie in
[0,1]
. We could rearrange the inequalities as follows.

\displaystyle(p^{a(1)}k_{1}-k_{2})t\geq p^{a(1)}\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha),

(267)

\displaystyle(p^{a(1)}k_{1}-k_{2})(1-t)\geq p^{a(1)}\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha).

The conditions force
t\in[0,1]
.

Case 2:
If
\#T=1
but
m_{1}\notin\{1,n-1\}
, then
a(1)
is
N
and
\alpha
satisfies

k_{1}\geq\alpha,\qquad p^{N}\alpha\geq k_{1},

(268)

and we must choose
t
such that

\displaystyle p^{N}\big(tk_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle\geq tk_{1},

(269)

\displaystyle p^{N}\big((1-t)k_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle\geq(1-t)k_{1}.

Similarly, taking the sum of the inequalities, we obtain

p^{N}\alpha\geq k_{1},

(270)

which follows from our assumption. By the same reason as the previous case, there exists some
t\in[0,1]
that satisfies both inequalities.

Case 3:
If
\#T=1
and
m_{1}=n-1
, then
a(1)=N
and
\alpha
satisfies

k_{1}\geq\alpha,\qquad(p^{N(n-m)}-1)\alpha\geq(p^{N(n-m-1)}-1)k_{1}.

(271)

Let
\delta=r_{\textnormal{tot}}-n_{1}>0
. By construction, we have
m^{0}_{1}=m-\delta
and
m^{s}_{1}=m+(s-1)\delta
. We look for some
t\in[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta))}-1)\big(tk_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)\geq(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta)-1)}-1)tk_{1},

(272)

\displaystyle(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-1)\big((1-t)k_{1}-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)\big)\geq(p^{N(n-(m-\delta)-1)}-1)(1-t)k_{1}.

This follows from a direct but complicated computation, which we present below for the integrality of the proof. The above inequalities are equivalent to

\displaystyle{\mdmathbb\displaystyle I}:(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta))}-p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta)-1)})tk_{1}\geq(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta))}-1)\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha),

(273)

\displaystyle{\mdmathbb\displaystyle II}:(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-p^{N(n-(m-\delta)-1)})(1-t)k_{1}\geq(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-1)\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha).

Taking
p^{Ns\delta}\mdmathbb{I}+\mdmathbb{II}
, we want to show

(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-p^{N(n-(m-\delta)-1)})k_{1}\geq\frac{(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-p^{Ns\delta})(p^{N}-1)p^{N(s-1)}+(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-1)(p^{N(s-1)}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha).

(274)

We could rearrange the terms and denote
q=p^{N}
for simplicity. We could simplify the inequalities to be

\mu\alpha\geq\nu k_{1},

(275)

where the coefficients for
\alpha,k_{1}
respectively are

\displaystyle\mu=(q^{n-m+\delta+s}-q^{s\delta+s}+q^{s\delta+s-1}-q^{n-m+\delta}-q^{s-1}+1),

(276)

\displaystyle\nu=(q^{n-m+s+\delta-1}-q^{n-m+\delta-1}-q^{s\delta+s}+q^{s\delta+s-1}-q^{s-1}+1).

We hope that this is automatically satisfied, i.e., we need

\frac{\mu}{\nu}\geq\frac{q^{n-m}-1}{q^{n-m-1}-1}.

(277)

One could expand both sides, and a direct but boring computation verifies the desired inequality (We need to use the fact
\delta\geq 1
and
s\geq 2
).

Case 3’:
If
\#T=1
and
m_{1}=1
, the proof for this case is essentially the same as above.
\hfill\square

Remark 7.30
.

In Construction
7.22
, it is possible that
m^{l_{0}}_{1}=m_{1}
(resp.
m^{0}_{1}=0
). In this case the bundle
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{l_{0}}_{1}}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}
) is simply
\omega_{1}
(resp.
0
). The corresponding line bundle
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)]
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)
) degenerates as the pullback of some automorphic line bundle
[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\{k_{i}\})]
from the underlying Shimura variety. It can be verified (by a case-by-case discussion) that the above computations is still valid in these boundary cases.

Return to our proof. For this complete curve
C
, we have

\displaystyle\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)]\cdot C\big)

(278)

\displaystyle=\big(\beta[h]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(\{tk_{i}\};\alpha^{s}_{1})]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{(1-t)k_{i}\};\alpha^{0}_{1})]\cdot C\big)

\displaystyle=\beta\big([h]\cdot C\big)+\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{s}_{1}}}(\{tk_{i}\};\alpha^{s}_{1})]\cdot C\big)+\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{(1-t)k_{i}\};\alpha^{0}_{1})]\cdot C\big)\geq 0.

Here, nonnegativity follows from the choice
\beta\geq 0
and induction. This shows that in the first case (i.e., in the case
r_{\textnormal{tot}}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m})>n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{N}
), the bundle
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)
is nef over
Z
.

It remains to deal with the latter case, that is, if

r_{\textnormal{tot}}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m})=\sum_{j=1}^{N}r_{j}=n_{1}+\dots+n_{N}.

This is the induction base of our argument in this section.

If the essential degree
t=1
, this is the induction base of our proof in this subsection, and we have shown that the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(k_{i};\alpha)
to
Z
is nef in Step 2.

Otherwise, we may assume the essential degree
t\geq 2
. Let
\tilde{Z}
be the closed subscheme of

Z\times_{X}Y^{N}_{m^{1}_{N}}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y^{2}_{m^{1}_{2}}

(279)

defined by the condition

\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{1}_{j}}\subseteq V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{m^{1}_{j+1}}),\ \forall 1\leq j\leq N.

(280)

Here the integers
m^{1}_{j}
are given in Construction
7.22
. Since the total slope takes the minimal possible value, we must have
m^{0}_{1}=m^{1}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}\subseteq V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}^{2,(p)}_{m^{1}_{2}})
. The above condition implies that the sequence

\{\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}=\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}},\mathcal{F}^{2}_{m^{1}_{2}},\mathcal{F}^{3}_{m^{1}_{3}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{N-1}_{m^{1}_{N-1}},\mathcal{F}^{N}_{m^{1}_{N}},\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m^{0}_{1}}\}

(281)

forms an
F_{es},V_{es}
-chain over
\tilde{Z}
. By Theorem
4.12
,
\tilde{Z}
admits a proper surjective morphism to an auxiliary unitary Shimura variety
X^{\prime}
.

Lemma 7.31
.

X^{\prime}
has essential degree
t^{\prime}=\#T_{X^{\prime}}\leq t=\#T_{X}
.

Proof
: It suffices to show that the essential degree does not increase. By Theorem
4.2
,
X^{\prime}
has signature
\{(m_{i}^{\prime},n_{i}^{\prime})\}
, where

m^{\prime}_{i}=n-r_{i-1}.

(282)

By the definition of slopes, if
m_{i}\in\{0,n\}
, then
r_{i}=0
. Hence

T_{X^{\prime}}\subseteq\big\{i|\ m_{i-1}\notin\{0,n\}\big\}.

(283)

Thus,
t^{\prime}\leq t
.
\hfill\square

Recall the inductive hypothesis at the beginning of this subsection: To prove the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)
of essential degree
t
, we need to assume that the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\lambda)
is known for any unitary Shimura variety
X^{\prime}
where
t^{\prime}\leq t
. Thus, the line bundle

\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\{k^{\prime}_{i}\})

(284)

on
X^{\prime}
is nef if and only if the weight
\{k_{i}\}
satisfy the condition in Theorem
7.4
. In particular,

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{N+1}p^{j-2}[\omega_{j}^{\prime}]=\sum_{j=1}^{N}p^{j-2}\Big(\frac{1}{p}[\frac{V\mathcal{F}^{j-1}_{m^{1}_{j-1}}}{p\mathcal{F}^{j,(p)}_{m^{1}_{j}}}]\Big)

(285)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{N+1}p^{j-2}\frac{1}{p}\Big([\frac{V\mathcal{F}^{j-1}_{m^{1}_{j-1}}}{VpH_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{j-1}}]+[\frac{VpH_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A/X)_{j-1}}{p\mathcal{F}^{j,(p)}_{m^{1}_{j}}}]\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{N+1}p^{j-2}\Big([\omega_{j}/\mathcal{F}^{j}_{m^{1}_{j}}]+\frac{1}{p}[\mathcal{F}^{j-1}_{m^{1}_{j-1}}]\Big)

\displaystyle=p^{N-1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]+\frac{1}{p}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]+[\omega_{2}]+p[\omega_{3}]+\cdots+p^{N-2}[\omega_{N}]

is nef. Since in the previous parts of this paper, we have presented many times how to compute the classes
[\omega_{j}^{\prime}]
from the moduli problem, we omit the details here. We remark that some of the
\omega^{\prime}_{j}
’s might have rank
0
or
n
, so their classes
[\omega_{j}]\in(\textnormal{Pic X'})_{\mdmathbb Q}
are 0. It is harmless to include them to maintain the integrality of the formula.
We have

\displaystyle=\frac{p}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)[\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(p^{N-1},1,\dots,p^{N-2})]+[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\lambda^{\prime})]

(286)

on
\tilde{Z}
. Here
\lambda^{\prime}=\{k_{i}^{\prime}\}
, where

\displaystyle k_{1}^{\prime}=k_{1}-\frac{p\cdot p^{N-1}}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha)=\frac{1}{p^{N}-1}(p^{N}\alpha-k_{1}),

(287)

\displaystyle k_{2}^{\prime}=k_{2}-\frac{p}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha),

\displaystyle k_{3}^{\prime}=k_{3}-\frac{p\cdot p}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha),

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots

\displaystyle k_{N}^{\prime}=k_{N}-\frac{p\cdot p^{N-2}}{p^{N}-1}(k_{1}-\alpha).

It is straightforward to verify all the inequalities
p^{a(i_{s})}k_{i_{s}}^{\prime}\geq k_{i_{s+1}}^{\prime}
for all
2\leq s\leq t
. For
s=1
, we have (recall that
i_{1}=1
)

p^{a(1)}k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{i_{2}}^{\prime}=p^{a(1)}k_{1}-k_{i_{2}}-\frac{p^{a(1)}}{p^{N}-1}(p^{N}-1)(k_{1}-\alpha)=p^{a(1)}\alpha-k_{i_{2}}\geq 0.

(288)

Therefore, we have expressed the restriction of
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)]
to
\tilde{Z}
as a summation of two nef line bundles, so it is also nef.

Consequently, we have proved the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(\{k_{i}\};\alpha)
over the minimal partial flag space.

7.6 Step 5: The sufficiency part of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\lambda)
with
i\notin T

In this section, we consider the line bundle

[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\lambda)]=[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{i_{1}},\dots,k_{i_{t}};\alpha)]=\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{j}[\omega_{i_{j}}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}],

where
Y^{i}_{m}
is a minimal partial flag space with
i\notin T
. We aim to prove the nefness of the above line bundle when the weight
\lambda
satisfies the condition in Theorem
7.3
. Our strategy is basically the same as the previous section —- We construct an auxiliary variety by consecutive blow-ups up to Frobenius twists and make use of the inductive hypothesis at the beginning of this section. Since most ideas are the same, we will omit some details, but make important remarks for the adjustments to the previous subsection.

For simplicity, we assume that
i_{1}=1\in T
,
i_{2}=i_{1}+a(i_{1})=1+a(1)
, and
i_{1}<i<i_{2}
. Our goal is to show

\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\lambda)\cdot C]\big)\geq 0

(289)

for any complete curve
C
(or equivalently, its pullback along any proper morphism).

Let
\pi:Z\to Y^{i}_{m}
be a proper morphism such that
\vec{r}(Z,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})=\vec{r}_{0}
. The induction hypothesis in subsection 7.1 in particular implies the following assumption.

Induction Hypothesis:

\bullet
The sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{l}}(\lambda)
, where
1\leq l<m
, holds for any proper variety over
Y^{i}_{l}
where
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{l}
has slope
\leq\vec{r}_{0}
. More precisely, if
\pi:W\to Y^{i}_{l}
is a proper morphism such that
\vec{r}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{l})\leq\vec{r}_{0}
, then
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{l}}(\lambda)
is nef provided that
\lambda
satisfies the condition given by Theorem
7.3
.

\bullet
The sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{l}}(\lambda)
, where
m\leq l\leq n
, holds for any proper variety over
Y^{i}_{l}
where
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{l}
has slope
<\vec{r}_{0}
. More precisely, if
\pi:W\to Y^{i}_{l}
is a proper morphism such that
\vec{r}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{l})<\vec{r}_{0}
, then
\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{l}}(\lambda)
is nef provided that
\lambda
satisfies the condition given by Theorem
7.3
.

As before, we again distinguish two cases:

•

Either
r_{\textnormal{tot}}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})>n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
,

•

or
r_{\textnormal{tot}}(W,\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
.

In the first case, we can construct the variety
\tilde{Z}
in the same manner as Construction
7.22
, but with the starting point of each step at
i
. At the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
-component, we obtain a sequence of integers

m^{0}_{i}<m^{1}_{i}<\cdots<m^{l_{0}}_{i}

together with a sequence

\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{0}_{i}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{1}_{i}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{l_{0}}_{i}}

(290)

of subbundles of
H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}
. As in Lemma
7.24
, the forgetful map
\tilde{Z}\to Z
remains proper and surjective. Moreover, our construction of "strata Hasse invariants"
{h}^{l}_{j}
and
h^{l}
for
1\leq l\leq l_{0}-1,1\leq j\leq N
on
\tilde{Z}
remains valid (except that we always start from the
\tilde{\tau}_{i}
-th component).

Let
C
be a complete curve on
\tilde{Z}
. We continue to let
s
denote the smallest positive integer such that
C
is contained in the zero locus of
h^{s}
. (As before, if
C
is not contained in any of the
Z(h^{k})
’s, we simply set
s=l_{0}
.)

We take

\displaystyle=[h^{s-1}]+\frac{p^{N}+1}{p^{N}}[h^{s-2}]+\cdots+\frac{p^{(s-2)N}+\cdots+1}{p^{(s-2)N}}[h^{1}]

(291)

\displaystyle=p^{N}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{s}_{i}}]-\frac{p^{sN}-1}{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{1}_{i}}]+\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{0}_{i}}].

Recall that
m^{1}_{i}=m
. We have the following linear combination

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{i_{1}},\dots,k_{i_{t}};\alpha)]

(292)

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{(s-2)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha[h]-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{s}_{i}}]-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{0}_{i}}]+\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{i_{j}}[\omega_{i_{j}}]

\displaystyle=\beta[h]+\bigg(-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{s}_{1}}]+\sum_{j=1}^{t}uk_{i_{j}}[\omega_{i_{j}}]\bigg)+\bigg(-\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m^{0}_{1}}]+\sum_{j=1}^{t}(1-u)k_{i_{j}}[\omega_{i_{j}}]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\beta[h]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m^{s}_{i}}}(uk_{i_{1}},\dots,uk_{i_{t}};\alpha^{s}_{i})]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m^{0}_{i}}}((1-u)k_{i_{1}},\dots,(1-u)k_{i_{t}};\alpha^{0}_{i})].

Here, since
i\notin T
, it would be better to keep track of the set of essential places. So we choose to only record these
\omega_{i_{j}}
’s in our formula. The key is again to prove the following proposition

Proposition 7.32
.

If we are not in the case
t=1,m_{1}=m=n-1
or
t=1,m_{1}=m=1
, then there exists a suitable choice of real numbers
\beta\geq 0
and
u\in[0,1]
fitting into the above equation, such that the weights for the line bundles
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m^{s}_{i}}}(\{uk_{j}\};\alpha^{s}_{1})]
and
[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m^{0}_{1}}}(\{(1-u)k_{j}\};\alpha^{0}_{1})]
satisfy their respective nefness conditions given by Theorem
7.3
.

Proof.
The number
\beta
is by definition nonnegative. The choice of
t
is again based on a case-by-case calculation. Since the ideas of our computations are similar as before, we explain the ideas of our proof in the following.

Case 1:
If
t\geq 2
, then
\alpha
satisfies

\alpha\geq 0,\qquad p^{a(1)}\big(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)\geq k_{2}.

(293)

We hope to choose
0\leq u\leq 1
such that

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big(uk_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\cdot\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha\big)\geq uk_{2},

(294)

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big((1-u)k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\cdot\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha\big)\geq(1-u)k_{2}.

A direct computation of the summation of two equations, similar to the previous step, implies the existence of
u
.

Case 2:
If
t=1
but
m_{1}\notin\{1,n-1\}
, then
a(1)=N
, and the arguments are the same as above, with all
k_{2}
’s replaced by
k_{1}
.

Case 3:
If
t=1
,
m_{1}=n-1
, and
m\neq n-1
, then
a(1)=N
and
\alpha
satisfies

\alpha\geq 0,\qquad\big(p^{N(n-m)}-1\big)(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)\geq\big(p^{N(n-m-1)}-1\big)k_{1}.

(295)

Recall that
\delta=r_{\textnormal{tot}}-n_{1}=r_{\textnormal{tot}}-1
, and that
m^{0}_{i}=m-\delta
,
m^{s}_{i}=m+(s-1)\delta
by construction. We hope to choose
0\leq u\leq 1
such that

\displaystyle(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta))}-1)\big(uk_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\cdot\frac{p^{(s-1)N}(p^{N}-1)}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha\big)\geq(p^{N(n-(m+(s-1)\delta)-1)}-1)uk_{1},

(296)

\displaystyle(p^{N(n-(m-\delta))}-1)\big((1-u)k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\cdot\frac{p^{(s-1)N}-1}{p^{sN}-1}\alpha\big)\geq(p^{N(n-(m-\delta)-1)}-1)(1-u)k_{1}.

The linear algebra argument then becomes essentially the same as in the previous subsection. We omit the detailed computations here.

Case 4:
If
\#T=1
,
m_{1}=1
, and
m\neq 1
, this follows dually from the above computation.

\hfill\square

Proposition 7.33
.

If
t=1
and
m_{1}=m=n-1
or
1
, then the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{1};\alpha)
holds.

Proof.
The nefness criterion in this case can be proved directly.

Case 5:
If
\#T=1
,
m_{1}=n-1
and
m=n-1
. Then
a(1)=N
and
\alpha
satisfies

\alpha\geq 0,\qquad k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\geq 0.

(297)

The condition differs from those in the previous cases. We prove that
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{n-1}}(k_{1},\dots,k_{t};\alpha)
is nef under the above condition.

Consider the stratum
\tilde{Z}
defined by

\tilde{Z}:=[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}\subseteq V^{-(i-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i,(p^{i-1})})]\subseteq Z\times_{Y^{i}_{n-1}}Y^{i}_{n-1}\times_{X}Y^{1}_{n-2}.

(298)

The forgetful map
\tilde{Z}\to Y^{i}_{n-1}
is proper and surjective, and in fact, it is a blow-up, since generically
V^{-(i-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i,(p^{i-1})})
intersects
\omega_{1}
in a vector bundle of rank
n-2
.

There exists a strata Hasse invariant on
\tilde{Z}
given by

h:\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}\longrightarrow H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{1}/V^{-1}_{es}(\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i,(p^{i-1})})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/X)_{i}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{n-1})^{\otimes p^{i-1}}.

(299)

The second map is induced by the Verschiebung map. The zero locus of
h
is the closed stratum defined by
\omega_{1}=V^{-(i-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i,(p^{i-1})})
. We have

[h]=-p^{i-1}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{n-1}]-[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}]

(300)

in
\textnormal{(Pic }\tilde{Z}\textnormal{)}_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. We consider the following linear combination

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{n-1}]

(301)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{p^{i-1}}[h]+k_{1}[\omega_{1}]+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha[\omega_{1}]-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{p^{i-1}}[h]+\big(k_{1}+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}]+k_{1}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{n-2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{p^{i-1}}[h]+[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{n-2}}(k_{1}+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha;k_{1})].

We can view the second factor as the pullback of a line bundle from the minimal partial flag space
Y^{1}_{n-2}
, and since
Y^{1}_{m}
is flagged at the essential set
T=\{1\}
, we have already proved the sufficiency part of the corresponding nefness criterion in the previous step. It is straightforward to check that

\displaystyle k_{1}+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\geq k_{1},\qquad(p^{2N}-1)k_{1}\geq(p^{N}-1)(k_{1}+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)

(302)

holds. So
[[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{n-2}}(k_{1}+\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha,k_{1})]
is a nef class. For a test curve
C\in\tilde{Z}
, we have

\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{n-1}}(k_{1};\alpha)]\cdot C\big)\geq 0

(303)

if
C\nsubseteq Z(h)
. If
C\subseteq Z(h)
, then the moduli interpretation of
h
implies that

[\omega_{1}]=[V^{-(i-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i,(p^{i-1})})]=p^{i-1}[\mathcal{F}_{n-1}^{i}]

(304)

on
\textnormal{Pic (}Z(h)\textnormal{)}_{\mdmathbb Q}
. Therefore,

\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{n-1}}(k_{1};\alpha)]\cdot C\big)=\big([\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{n-1}}(k_{1};\alpha)]|_{Z(h)}\cdot C\big)=\big((k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)[\omega_{1}]\cdot C\big)\geq 0.

(305)

This completes the proof of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y_{n-1}^{i}}(k_{1};\alpha)
.

Case 6:
If
\#T=1
,
m_{1}=1
, and
m=1
, this follows dually from the above proof.

\hfill\square

It remains to deal with the case when the pullback of
\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}
to
Z
has slope
r_{\textnormal{tot}}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
.

Proposition 7.34
.

If
r_{\textnormal{tot}}(Z,\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{N}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\{k_{j}\};\alpha)
is nef over
Z
as long as the coefficients satisfy the condition of Theorem
7.3
.

Proof.
Similar to the proof in the previous subsection, we apply the induction hypothesis and use the geometric description Theorem
4.12
.

Case 1:
Either
t=\#T\geq 2
, or
t=1
but
m_{1}\notin\{1,n-1\}
.

We need to show that the pullback of

[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{i_{1}},\dots,k_{i_{t}};\alpha)]=\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{i_{j}}[\omega_{i_{j}}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]

to
Z
is nef under the condition: If
t\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\alpha\geq 0,

(306)

\displaystyle p^{a(i_{j})}k_{i_{j}}\geq k_{i_{j+1}},\qquad\textnormal{for $2\leq j\leq t$},

\displaystyle p^{a(i_{1})}\big(k_{i_{1}}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)\geq k_{i_{2}}.

If
t=1
:

\alpha\geq 0,\qquad p^{a(i_{1})}\big(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)\geq k_{1}.

(307)

Let
{u}_{i}=m
, and for
1\leq j\leq N-1
, we define inductively

{u}_{i-j}={u}_{i-j+1}+n_{i-j+1}-r_{j}.

(308)

Here, we view the subscripts of
u
as modulo
N
. Our choice here is consistent with the previous subsection. Consider the closed subscheme
\tilde{Z}
defined by the condition

\tilde{Z}:=[\mathcal{F}^{j}_{u_{j}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1,(p)}_{u_{j+1}}),\textnormal{ for all $1\leq j\leq N$})]

(309)

of the variety

Z\times_{Y^{i}_{m}}Y^{1}_{u_{1}}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y^{N}_{u_{N}}.

(310)

The projection map
\tilde{Z}\to Z
is proper and surjective, for the same reason as before. Thus, it suffices to prove that the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{1},\dots,k_{t};\alpha)
to
\tilde{Z}
is nef. By definition, the sequence

\mathcal{F}^{1}_{u_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{N}_{u_{N}}

(311)

forms an
F,V
-chain.

By Proposition
4.12
, we obtain a proper surjective morphism (up to Frobenius twists)
\tilde{Z}\to X^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime}
is an auxiliary unitary Shimura variety of signature
(m^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i})
. Let
T^{\prime}
denote the essential set of
X^{\prime}
.

Lemma 7.35
.

Let
t^{\prime}=\#T^{\prime}
. Then
t^{\prime}\leq t+1
.

Proof:
For
1<j<N
, if
m_{i-j}\in\{0,n\}
, then
r_{j}=0
and
\mathcal{F}_{{u}_{i-j}}^{i-j}=V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i-j+1,(p)}_{{u}_{i-j+1}})
. This follows directly from the definition of slopes and
{u}_{i-j}
. So for
s\neq i
, if
m_{s}=0
or
n
, then
m^{\prime}_{s+1}=n
. Since

T=\{i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{t}\}.

(312)

This implies that

T^{\prime}\subseteq\{i_{1}+1,i+1,i_{2}+1,\dots,i_{t}+1\}.

(313)

Hence
t^{\prime}\leq t+1
.
\hfill\square

Back to our proof of the proposition. The above lemma implies that we could apply the induction hypothesis at the beginning of this subsection. We know that
\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(k^{\prime}_{1},\dots,k^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}})
is nef as long as the coefficients satisfy the corresponding condition in Theorem
7.4
. Consider the class (recall that
u_{i}=m
)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}p^{j}[\omega_{i+j+1}^{\prime}]=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}p^{j}\cdot\frac{1}{p}\big[\frac{V\mathcal{F}^{i+j}_{u_{i+j}}}{p\mathcal{F}^{i+j+1,(p)}_{u_{i+j+1}}}\big]

(314)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}p^{j}\cdot\big(\frac{1}{p}[\mathcal{F}^{i+j}_{u_{i+j}}]+[\omega_{i+j+1}/\mathcal{F}^{i+j+1}_{u_{i+j+1}}]\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]+\sum_{j=0}^{N-2}p^{j}[\omega_{i+j+1}]+p^{N-1}[\omega_{i}/\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}].

As before, some
\omega_{s}
’s might have rank
0
or
n
. It is non-problematic to keep them in the formula and put some coefficients
k_{s}
before them. We omit the computation for the class
[\omega_{s}^{\prime}]
via Dieudonné theory.

Now (recall that
i_{1}=1
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{t}k_{i_{j}}[\omega_{i_{j}}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]

(315)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}[\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime})]+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\big(k_{i_{j}}-\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot p^{i_{j}-i-1}\big)[\omega_{i_{j}}]+\big(k_{1}-\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot p^{N-i}\big)[\omega_{1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}[\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime})]+[\mathcal{L}_{X}(\tilde{\lambda})].

Here
\tilde{\lambda}
corresponds to the tuple

(\tilde{k}_{i_{1}},\dots,\tilde{k}_{i_{t}})=(k_{1}-\frac{\alpha p^{N-i}}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}},k_{i_{2}}-\frac{\alpha p^{i_{2}-i-1}}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}},\dots,k_{i_{t}}-\frac{\alpha p^{i_{t}-i-1}}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}).

(316)

It suffices to check that the latter is a nef class. For
2\leq j\leq t
, we see that

p^{a(i_{j})}\tilde{k}_{i_{j}}\geq\tilde{k}_{i_{j+1}}.

(317)

Moreover (recall that
i_{2}-1=a(1)
),

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big(k_{1}-\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot p^{N-i}\big)\geq k_{i_{2}}-\frac{\alpha}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot p^{i_{2}-i-1}

(318)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}k_{1}-\frac{p^{N+a(1)-i}-p^{i_{2}-i-1}}{p^{N-1}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\alpha\geq k_{i_{2}}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}k_{1}-p^{a(1)+1-i}\alpha\geq k_{i_{2}}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle p^{a(1)}\big(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)\geq k_{i_{2}},

which holds by our assumption.

Case 2:
We have
t=\#T=1
,
m_{1}=n-1
, but
m\neq n-1
.

In this case, we need to show that the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{1};\alpha)=k_{1}[\omega_{1}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]
to
Z
is nef under the condition

\alpha\geq 0,\qquad(p^{N(n-m)}-1)(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)\geq(p^{N(n-m-1)}-1)k_{1}.

(319)

We could again construct the auxiliary variety
\tilde{Z}
and the
F,V
-chain

\mathcal{F}^{1}_{u_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{F}^{N}_{u_{N}}.

(320)

Note that
r_{\textnormal{tot}}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}n_{j}=1
in this case. Thus, all the entries in the tuple
\vec{r}(\tilde{Z},\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m})=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N})
are 0, except a single 1. By Lemma
6.8
,
r_{j}\leq n_{i-j}
for
1\leq j<N
. The only possible nonzero terms are
r_{i-1}
and
r_{N}
.

If
r_{i-1}=0
and
r_{N}=1
. Then
u_{1}=u_{2}=\cdots=u_{i}=m
, and the inclusion conditions

\mathcal{F}^{i-v}_{u_{i-v}}\subseteq V^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{i-v+1,(p)}_{u_{i-v+1}})

(321)

for
1\leq v\leq N-1
are, in fact, equalities. In particular,
u_{1}=m
and

\mathcal{F}^{1}_{u_{1}}=V^{-(i-1)}(\mathcal{F}_{m}^{i,(p^{i-1})}).

(322)

In
(\textnormal{Pic $\tilde{Z}$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
, we deduce that

[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]={p^{i-1}}[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}].

(323)

So we have

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}]-\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]=k_{1}[\omega_{1}]-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]

(324)

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]+\big(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha\big)[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m}]

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m}}(k_{1};k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)].

Note that
\tilde{Z}
admits a proper morphism to
Y^{1}_{m}
, so the last equality means that we can view the above class as the pullback of an automorphic line bundle from
Y^{1}_{m}
. Note that
Y^{1}_{m}
is flagged at the essential set
T=\{1\}
, and

(p^{N(n-m)}-1)(k_{1}-\frac{1}{p^{i-1}}\alpha)\geq(p^{N(n-m-1)}-1)k_{1}.

(325)

Since we have already proved the sufficiency part of the nefness criterion for
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\mu)
, we deduce that
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{1};\alpha)|_{Z}
is nef.

If
r_{i-1}=1
and
r_{N}=0
. Then
u_{1}=u_{2}=\cdots=u_{i-1}=m-1
and
u_{i}=\cdots=u_{N}=m
. Hence,

\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}=V^{-(N+1-i)}(\mathcal{F}_{m-1}^{1,(p^{N+1-i})}).

(326)

By Lemma
5.2
, we have

[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]=-p^{N+1-i}[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m-1}]

(327)

in
(\textnormal{Pic $\tilde{Z}$})_{\mdmathbb{Q}}
. So

\displaystyle=k_{1}[\omega_{1}]+p^{N+1-i}\alpha[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m-1}]

(328)

\displaystyle=\big(k_{1}+p^{N+1-i}\alpha\big)[\omega_{1}/\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m-1}]+k_{1}[\mathcal{F}^{1}_{m-1}]

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{1}_{m-1}}(k_{1}+p^{N+1-i}\alpha;k_{1})].

It suffices to check

(p^{N(n-m+1)}-1)k_{1}\geq(p^{N(n-m)}-1)(k_{1}+p^{N+1-i}\alpha).

(329)

This is straightforward. (The case
m=1
is a little bit different but much easier. In this case, the condition is
[\mathcal{F}^{i}_{m}]=-p^{N+1-i}[\omega_{1}]
. We have

[\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(k_{1};\alpha)]=(k_{1}+p^{N+1-i}\alpha)[\omega_{1}].

(330)

The nefness follows directly from the nefness of the Hodge line bundle
[\omega_{1}]
.) This completes the proof in Case 2.

Case 2’:
If
r=\#T=1
and
m_{1}=1
. This follows from a dual argument.

By the above case-by-case discussion, we have completed the proof of Proposition
7.34
.
\hfill\square

Combining all the discussions above, we have shown the nefness of the automorphic line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{Y^{i}_{m}}(\{k_{j}\};\alpha)
over minimal partial flag spaces, under the numerical assumption of Theorem
7.3
.

Combining the arguments in Section 7, we have completed the proof of the ampleness criterion for automorphic line bundle over unitary Shimura varieties, i.e., Theorem
3.24
,
3.25
,
3.26
,
3.27
.
\hfill\square
